**2026-10-11, the loop editor while it plays; Transpose; the voicing's snap; a ratchet's held last hit (0.18 dev,
built, not flashed).** The owner, after using the sequencer on the unit: "In the sequencer if playback is going on then
when I enter keys to a selected step, it also ends up combining the playing notes into that step. In this case it
should only enter the keys I'm playing, not the notes playing from the sequence. Also the name of the chord changes
during playback, I would like this to always show chord/notes of the selected step. Then during playback in the
sequencer, show the name of the playing step chord/notes to the left of the Page Name (don't offset the page name, also
don't show voicing here since that would overlap the page name probably) in blue. Also in sequencer on the second trig
page for Knob 4 which is empty, let's make this a transpose ... For the voicing follow selection, below -12 is fine,
but let's make it so you can hold GLO key and turn voicing to snap to Follow or 0. Ratchet playing inside the step is
fine, but let's make it so if length is greater than one step, the last trig of ratchet then holds for the length."

*The notes in the step.* The loop's own notes could not be found entering a step, and cannot: the editor writes what
the engine's gesture hook reports, a loop voice never raises it, and MIDI in plays the parts, not the chord engine.
Tried on main with the loop playing and a chord held over its chords: no latch, the arp, Advanced, Key Mode with Split
Oct (the owner's settings record of 2026-10-10), Split Bass, LOCK: the step got the played chord alone each time. What
did put a chord that was not being played into a step, and makes the name line change during playback, is **a latch**
(LATCH / Sticky, or a Perform mode's Hold): a latched chord does not end when its keys go up, the editor wrote at the
gesture's end, so the chord arrived at the next press, in whichever step was selected then, while the latched chord,
still sounding over the loop, was what the name line showed between the loop's notes. Taken as the cause; if the owner
had no latch on, it is not found. The fix is at the cause: the engine reports a latched gesture's key-up
(`CR_G_KEYUP`), the editor writes then. Free play style's re-sounded chord (a chord key with no root held) is still
written, as it sounds; left alone.

*The choices.* The name line: the selected step's always; the played chord only while a root key is down (the
player's own voice, `cr_snap.pl_*`). The playing name: what the loop STRUCK last (`crl.hit_*`, not the step's stored
events: a step silenced by its condition or chance is not named), kept until the next step that sounds rather than
blanked on empty steps (a lone chord would flash for a sixteenth), empty before the first note and when stopped;
left-aligned at x 6, 78 px at most, 13 / 11 / 10 px bold by fit (`G#madd4 JAZZ` at 10 px, whole); on the Trig page it
takes the left neighbour's place ("Step" is not drawn while a name shows; the alternative, two lines at the left, was
not built). Transpose: 0 .. 127 for every note of the step or the detent is refused whole; chromatic with Key Mode on;
the cell the root note, hot: the turn's sum. The snap: Follow <-> 0 from either by a turn either way, from another
value clockwise 0, the other way Follow; inside the editor it takes GLO + ALGORITHM from the bass level. The ratchet:
`last = Note len - (N - 1) slices` for a note longer than a step, worked out when the event fires (`pv[].last`, in the
struct's padding); the record's format is untouched by all of it (Transpose rewrites roots; nothing new is stored).
**A saved loop with a ratchet on a note longer than a step now sounds that note on** (it was silent after its step).

Cost: flash +784 B (XIP 455460 -> 456244), POOL +20 B (314744 -> 314764, 91.5 %: `lm_now` 16, the draw cache 4), RAM
+0 (80612 B, 82.0 %: the new bytes fell in padding). The ISR: three byte stores and one test a hit. A UI frame on the
emulator over 4.2 s of the editor playing (estimates, `cr_loop_edplay.txt`): the average +2.8 %, the worst +8 %; not
counted on the unit. Tests: `tests/cr_loop_test.c` `s_ratlong` (the two examples and x3 on 2 steps in exact ticks at x1
and x1/16, a step and under as before, over the next step, across the loop's end, a stop inside the held hit, the
map), `s_transpose`, `s_edkeys` (no latch, LATCH, Arp with and without Hold: 31 of the new checks fail on 86d162f);
`tools/emu/scripts/cr_loop_edplay.txt` in `test_cr.sh`; `tests/cr_draw_test.c` (the playing name: blue, nothing else
moves, the longest names whole, the Trig page, one strip, the slide; the Transpose cell). The manual's two editor
pictures regenerated. Not tested: anything on the unit (the blue name's legibility on the panel, the feel of the
snap); the owner's actual settings at the time of his report; 3/4 and 6/8; the playing name with a take longer than
64 steps beyond the shared code; Free play style in the editor.

**2026-10-12, GLO + REC steps the record mode; the home read-out names the engine (0.18 dev, built, not flashed).** The
owner: "For your GLO + SEQ metronome shortcut, that is fine, I would also add shortcut for GLO + REC which changes the
record mode, cycling through them", and: "From the main root home pages when changing presets, I would like everything
to stay the same but show above the preset number in orange the current engine name. Then at the bottom it currently
says 'ENGINE - 20/25', let's change that to say 'Hold OPT to change engine', the numbers and the bar already
communicate how many presets there are and which one is selected."
*GLO + REC* (`cr_ui.c cu_rec_mode_next`, called from `cu_btn_press` beside GLO + SEQ, under the same condition: not
the sound editor): the next of Overwrite, Advance, Overdub, Replace, Step, wrapping, `LC_MODE` posted as the PLAY
menu's knob does, the message `Rec: <mode>`. GLO + REC had no function before (REC as the second button of a combo is
swallowed: no record, no undo). What the knob does during a capture was looked up first: nothing special, it sets the
mode with no refusal and no message, and `cr_loop_rec` reads the mode only with nothing captured; the shortcut does
the same. In the loop editor it steps the mode and clears no step: safe, the mode is a setting and the editor refuses
to open during a capture.
*The read-out* (`cu_pool_popup`): on the home pages (`cu_pool_home`: no layer, not the sound editor, not the save
dialog, which is exactly where OPT + PRESETS works) the engine stands over the number in the role orange (a meter's
`line` / `line_col`, drawn by `cr_p_meter` at 15 px bold on the panel's row 26) and the label is the fixed text. Two
decisions the request did not spell out: **the bass part's text is not the owner's**, because it would be false there
(OPT + ALGORITHM is the bass level, OPT + PRESETS changes the chord engine): it reads `BASS + EDIT, hold EDIT: engine`,
the true way, and Bass Behaviour Solo's ` · solo`, which the old label carried, moved to the engine's line
(`VA · solo`); and **outside the home pages the read-out is unchanged** (`FM6 · 05/25`, nothing over the number: the
engine picker's preview, a program change with a layer open), since the text would be false there too. `00 off` is
as it was. The trace keeps its `popup: part ...` line with the place (the tests count them) and adds
`home read-out: part N over '..' line '..'`; the emulator's frame line ends `over '..'`.
Widths at 14 px: `Hold OPT to change engine` 180 px, the bass line 206 px, of 240: no ellipsis. Mock-ups: `over` /
`overCol` in the meter's JSON (`make_mockups.py`, `make_preset_mockups.py`, `gen_cr_screens.py`); the designer does
not draw `over` and the PNG sheets were not rendered again. Cost: flash +416 B, RAM +16 B (the read-out's two texts),
POOL unchanged (314744 B). Tests: `tools/emu/scripts/cr_glo_rec.txt`, `cr_pool_hint.txt` (in `test_cr.sh`),
`tests/cr_draw_test.c` (seven variants: the line's place, colour, the texts whole, no overlap; the picker's without
it); `docs/manual/sound-popup.png` regenerated. Not tested: anything on the unit (the orange line's legibility on its
panel, the feel of GLO + REC with the real buttons' timing); GLO + REC in the calibration and in SAFE MODE; the
read-out after a SysEx `--select` (it needs the device tool's path; it goes through the same `cu_sound_popup`).

**2026-10-12, a major triad reads `CM`; OPT + ALGORITHM is the bass engine; BASS + ALGORITHM the bass level (0.18 dev,
built, not flashed).** The owner: "I noticed that for Major chords, nothing shows, major chords should use capital M
to differentiate from single notes. So C major would read CM", then, while it was being built, "Big Chord Name on home
page should also change"; and "Bass presets, let's make that work similar to the main presets, GLO + Bass knob to
change engine. With this change then map, hold the ENV/Bass key and turn bass knob for the bass level shortcut."
*The name.* One function names a chord for every screen, `cr_chord_name` (`cr_engine.c`): the home views' big name
(`cr_chord_info`), the arp band, the loop editor's name line, its playing name, step entry's line, the trace. The
change is one line there: quality `CR_Q_MAJ` with no extension bit gives the quality text `M` (at the quality's size,
0.58 of the root's, on the root's line, as `m`). The rule for the rest, decided here: a major triad WITH an extension
keeps Orchid's name (`C7`, `CM7`, `C6`, `C9`, `C6 9`, `CM7 9`, `CJAZZ`, `CWTF`): none of those can be read as a note
(no screen prints an octave after a chord's root), `CM7` must stay the major seventh rather than become "CM" + "7" =
dominant, and `CM6 9` would be longer for nothing. `C5` (power), `C+`, `Csus`, `Cdim`, `Cm`, `Cm add4` are as they
were; `C` alone is `CR_Q_NONE`, a single note. So only one name of the 8 x 16 changes. Display only: the engine's
spec, the loop events (root, quality << 4 | extension) and the MIDI are untouched. Widths: the new name is the
shortest chord name there is after the note itself (`G#M`: narrower than `G#m`'s neighbours `G#dim`, `G#sus`), so no
place gets a longer longest name: the big name is not squeezed, the playing name stays at its 13 px face, the name
line beside `step 64/64` is whole; 0 lint findings, the 28 ellipsised texts are the PLAY menu pickers' neighbours as
before.
*The bass engine* (`cu_epk_step(part, s)`, `cu_epk_commit`): the chord part's picker with a part: `cu.epk_on` 1 the
chord's, 2 the bass's (orange, "bass engine · OPT + ALGORITHM"); the same list (`cu_engine_at`: the shown melodic
engines, so a part standing on a retired engine's stand-in starts from the first), the same wrap, the same landing
(`cs.pool_pos[part]`, else the first preset), on the same pages (`cu_pool_home`). Decided here: a bass that is off
stays off (`cs.bass_sound` 0 or BASS tapped off: the engine and its sound are loaded and wait; the read-out shows
where it landed, not `00 off`); the other knob turned in the same hold commits the first pick and opens the other
picker. The home read-out's bass line is `Hold OPT to change engine` now (`CU_POOL_HINT_BASS` is gone).
*The bass level* (`cu_bass_level_hold`). Until now OPT + ALGORITHM, by one rule everywhere but the loop editor.
**The collision**: BASS held + a knob already meant something: any knob turned during a hold opens the button's layer
at once (`cu_activity`) and LOCKS it, and ALGORITHM, which the BASS layer does not claim, then fell through to its
own job: BASS held + ALGORITHM opened the BASS layer and changed the bass SOUND. The layer already has a Level cell
(KNOB 4). The mapping chosen: BASS held + ALGORITHM is the level always, and nothing is dropped (ALGORITHM alone still
turns the bass sound, in the layer too; KNOB 3 there as well): with no layer open and BASS not yet past the hold time
it is the old popup and NO layer (`cu.blvl`: the hold is spent, no layer at the hold time, no tap on release; a
shortcut that left the layer locked open would cost a HOME every time), and anything else touched in that hold opens
the layer as always (KNOB 1 is never the chord's voicing); with the BASS layer open (BASS held past the hold time,
held again in its locked layer, or held over another layer, which it replaces) the level moves in the layer, its
Level cell hot, and never the sound: the rule of "a knob turned while a layer's button is held acts in that layer",
which this combination broke until now. Not in the loop editor (the voicing) nor in the sound editor without a layer
(ENV is the editor's own button there). OPT + ALGORITHM off the home pages (the PLAY menu, the other layers, the sound
editor, the save dialog), where it was the level by the old rule, is ALGORITHM's own job there now, as OPT + PRESETS
always was: the PLAY menu's record mode, the bass sound elsewhere; the loop editor keeps Follow / 0.
Nothing new is stored. Mock-ups: `make_mockups.py` / `make_loop_mockups.py` name their major triads `FM` / `GM`
(the JSONs regenerated; five other JSONs picked up the "SEQ" label their generators already had); the PNG sheets
`design/choralroot-fm1-screens.png` and `choralroot-fm1-loop-screens.png` were not rendered again and show `F` / `G`.
No manual picture changed (none shows a major triad's name: `chord.png` is Dm7, the loop editor's selected step Am).
Tests: `tests/cr_engine_test.c` (the table; 8 qualities x 16 masks x 12 roots: no chord is a bare root, `M` only for
the plain triad), `tests/cr_draw_test.c` (the big name `G#M` on every named screen, the three looper places),
`tools/emu/scripts/cr_bass_engine.txt` in `test_cr.sh`, and the expectations that named a major triad. Not tested:
anything on the unit; the `M`'s legibility at the Notes / Scope views' small name; BASS + ALGORITHM over the save
dialog and in SAFE MODE.

**2026-10-12, FM TONE's Drive is the sound's (0.18 dev, built, not flashed).** The owner, asked whether FM TONE's AMP
Drive should read per cent like the Sends page: "FM TONE Drive, no, keep as 127 to match Digitone. Also Digitone
presets should save and load the drive level with them and also always enable drive if this is greater than 0 on FM
TONE and FX is off." Two merges earlier that cell had been made the chord part's drive send (one store, gated by FX
off, not an edit of the sound, never loaded).

What the formats held: FM TONE's 80-byte patch has no drive and gets none. A factory preset's row has one
(`QUAD_PRESETS`' `FX(drive, ..)`: 10 on WOBBLE, 0 on the other fifteen; our own values, not Digitone conversions), a
user record has the `P_DIST` field every save wrote, the session has the part's `p[]`. So nothing stored changed: the
drive is read back. `cr_ui.c`: while a part's engine is FM TONE its track's `P_DIST` is the sound's Drive.
`cu_drive_load` (called by `cu_load`, `cu_load_user`, `cu_init_load`, `cu_engine_switch` before they write the engine)
sets it from the preset or the record; `cu_fx_apply` leaves it with FX off; `cu_send` / `cu_send_set` read and write
it for such a part (the AMP cell, the Sends page, the Drive page, KNOB 4, the editor's FX group: one value), mark the
sound edited and do not switch FX on; `cu_fx_cells` keeps that Drive cell live with FX off. `fx.c`, the patch, the
stores and the records are untouched.

Decisions taken here, the owner's to overrule: (1) the part's own drive send is **remembered**, not overwritten: the
chord part's stays in `cs.fx_amt[]`, the bass part's (which lives in its track) is put aside in `cu_bdrive` while the
part is on FM TONE and the session's tag 29 stores it from there; a driven FM TONE preset therefore leaves nothing on
the next FM6 sound, and no stored field was added. (2) The Sends page's Drive knob on an FM TONE part **is** an edit
of the sound, as the AMP cell is. (3) Turning it with FX off leaves FX off. (4) With FX off only that cell is live;
the label still says `fx · off`. (5) The engine picker's switch to FM TONE takes 01 EP's Drive (0), INIT 0.

Old records: a user preset of 0.14 .. 0.17 or of an earlier 0.18 build loads with the byte in its `P_DIST`: the
part's drive when it was saved, 0 on the chord part if FX was off then. An earlier 0.18 build wrote the part's send
of that moment, so such a preset can carry a drive that was set for another sound: the owner's unit was not read.
An older session's FM TONE part comes up with what sounded when it was saved.

Bit-exact: all 153 scripts of main's `tools/emu/scripts/` rendered with `--wav` through an emulator built from
`git archive` of main (81331b4) and through this tree's: 153 identical WAVs (700 MB); none of them plays an FM TONE
preset with a drive, which is the one thing that changed. Felucca's goldens (`tests/run_tests.sh`) unchanged, `fx.c`
not edited. Tests: `cr_quad_drive.txt`, `cr_quad_drive_wav_{tone,fm6}_{hi,lo}.txt` (`test_cr.sh`),
`quad_drive_set.txt` / `quad_drive_check.txt` (`test_persist.sh`), `tests/cr_fm6_test.c` (ten "drive:" checks);
`cr_fx_sends.txt`'s FM TONE step now expects the sound's Drive. The emulator's dump has a `drive:` line. Not tested:
anything on the unit; a real preset saved by 0.14 .. 0.17 (an older record's layout is built by hand in the host
test); a backup restored over FM TONE parts; the web manager's sound import of an FM TONE record (it goes through
the same record and `cu_load_user`). No screen's layout changed; no manual picture was regenerated. Cost against
main's build: flash +512 B (.text 442432 -> 442944), RAM and POOL unchanged (POOL 314764 B).

## 2026-10-12 (the output chain: GitHub issue 3)

3y17. **0.19 dev (built; not flashed, not heard on the unit).** The report: "muffled / harsh compared to other
firmwares ... sloop sounds a lot clearer, fuller ... maybe it is just level matching / in the output stage" (a user on
0.17 or older, headphones). The owner: ChoralRoot sounds very good to him, he wants it improved in 0.19, and the
speaker must not end up noticeably quieter. Everything measured and decided is in docs/AUDIO-QUALITY.md; in short:

- **Against Sloop** (all three firmwares rendered on the host): the same chain, the same output at equal part level.
  The differences that can explain the report on 0.17 are level (single notes a median 9.4 dB under Sloop's at the same
  MASTER: part LEVEL 92 against 104, and Sloop's trims), mono (the same word in both ears on 164 of 168 sounds; Sloop's
  chorus and reverb return in stereo) and the mono chorus's comb. Not the FX amounts (Sloop is as wet), not the
  limiter or the clip (Sloop's are driven far harder), not the DAC (the four "codec" pins of the HAL are the I2S bus;
  the set-up is the same in every Felucca-family firmware; the chip is not identified).
- **Options > Output: Classic / Clean** (`fx.c`; bit 1 of the settings block's `pick_roots`, no new version; default
  Clean in the dev builds; switched with an 11.6 ms fade each way). Classic is 0.18's master: 161 emulator scripts
  bit for bit against a build of 19b6453 (`tools/emu/classic_exact.sh`), the goldens unchanged. Clean: the mix at
  x 0.75 kept in Q17, a limiter at 26000 that holds its peak 40 ms (Classic's gain moved 0.46 dB with every period of
  a 40 Hz note), a knee that is linear to 24576, MASTER last into the DAC's 24-bit word. Measured: a 6-note chord with
  the bass was in gain reduction 100 % of the time with dist -28 dB and is 0 .. 30 % with -41 .. -99 dB; a single
  note -43 .. -50 dB and now -76 .. -90; at MASTER -24 dB a quiet note's error -32 .. -48 dB and now -72 .. -86. Loud
  chords come out 0.4 .. 1.4 LU louder, single notes 2.4 LU quieter (the trim). About 5 us a half more on the unit
  (counted: 83 instructions a frame against 76 idle, 99 against 92 limiting).
- **Found on the way: Felucca's SPEAKER setting (LOWCUT / BASS+) survived a flash to ChoralRoot** and ran in the
  master with no control and no reset (the coordinator's lead, verified: a Felucca 1.0+ settings object is accepted;
  reproduced on 0.18's emulator with a seeded flash: -17 dB under 100 Hz). Held off since this build
  (`cr_settings_boot`, a restore, Reset Settings; the next save stores 0). It thins the bass, so it is not the report's
  cause.
- **Proposed, not default**: `CR_OUT_MAKEUP=1 ./build.sh` gives Clean's 2.5 dB back in the DAC word's unused upper
  6 dB (fm1-x0x uses all 6 as an option). Nobody has heard this unit up there; with it USB's master pair can clip.
- **Left for the owner's ear**: Output's default, the trim, the part LEVEL against Sloop's, the chorus's mono return.

The harness of the first measurement (it lived untracked in a worktree) is in the tree: `tests/master_measure.c`,
`tools/master_analyze.py`, `tools/master_measure_run.sh`, `tools/master_measure_tables.sh`, `tools/master_listen.py`.
Listening files and tables: `../audio-0.19/`. Not run: the browser emulator's test (no emcc on this Mac); nothing on
the device.

## 2026-10-12 (CZ-1's pops)

3y18. **The owner: "CZ-1 can pop and dropout when holding chords without FX, I believe it is an engine issue ... probably
internal clipping."** Looked for in the host render with a new tool (`tests/cz_pops.c`: INIT TONE and the 64 x six
scenarios x four chords x four roots x two velocities x the CZ-1 bass off / on, 24 960 runs, the sanitizer on every
int32, the peaks of every stage, each line's gain across block edges, jumps against the signal's own slope, holes).
No wrap and no saturation anywhere: it is not clipping. Two steps of the engine's own making were found and fixed:
(A) `cz_note_on` cleared a sounding voice's envelopes (the same note again, a chord change's common tone, MONO, the
arp), so its level fell to 0 between two samples and the attack began again: all 65 tones, in 4000 of 4160 re-strike
runs; a sounding voice now keeps its envelopes' levels and its DCW depth. (B) the DCW depth was a control block's and
stepped the phase function at the block's edge: a click 3 to 4 ms into a low note with a fast DCW attack (the CZ-1
bass at C2 under any chord: 14 000 of the dry mix); the depth is now ramped a sample at a time in a block where it
changed (`cz_native_line_mv`, `CZ_RECIP`), and a block where it did not runs the code it ran. After: 0 gain steps, 0
holes, 41 runs with a jump left (five percussive tones at C2: their own edges). A plain held chord showed no pop in
any run before either, apart from (B) at its start: what the owner hears on a held chord may be late audio, which no
host render shows; `tools/cz_pop_check.py` plays the worst case on the unit and reads SysEx 78 PEAK (not run: only
the owner runs device tools). Cost from the device's listing: +0.8 % a voice with the depth at rest, +9 % while it
moves, no divide in a loop; flash +15.8 KB (XIP 79.9 -> 82.7 %), RAM and POOL unchanged. Bit for bit: 158 of the 161
emulator scripts' WAVs against a build of 19b6453 (the three others play CZ-1), 132 of 197 goldens; the 65 CZ-1
goldens were rewritten, and with both fixes switched off in a copy all 197 are main's. Also measured, not changed:
CZ-1 is 5.4 dB hotter at the peak than the VA for the same chord (it adds a half-scale voice `* 4` where `dsp.c`'s
convention is `<< 1`); -6 dB for the engine would level it, a decision for the master's rework. Details, the tables
and what was not tested: docs/CZ1.md "Pops (0.19)". The ten worst, before and after: `../audio-0.19/cz-pops/`.

## 2026-10-12 (USB recording at 48 kHz)

3y19. **0.19 dev (built; not flashed, nothing run on a host).** The owner wants SLOOP 2.5's "USB audio at 48 kHz
too" (after Felucca 1.1.5) in ChoralRoot, as a compatibility feature: the synth and the headphones stay at 44.1 kHz.
**Read:** SLOOP's working tree at v2.6 (`firmware/src/usb.c` `FELUCCA_UAC_48K`, `uac_fir.h`, `tools/gen_uac_fir.py`,
`tests/uac_test.c`). **Not read:** Felucca 1.1.5 itself and SLOOP's v2.4.1..v2.5 diff: the agent's sandbox refuses
git commands aimed at other repositories, and Felucca's working tree is v1.0.1; what is said of Felucca's filter (24
taps, Q30, 7680 B) is SLOOP's own account of it. **What they shipped:** one alternate setting whose type I format
lists two discrete rates, the endpoint's `SET_CUR` choosing, `bcdDevice` raised (SLOOP 3.10 -> 3.20), a polyphase
FIR x 160 / 147 in the audio ISR's tap writing 48 kHz frames into the ring, packets of 48 frames, 47 or 49 by the
ring's fill; both stereo. **ChoralRoot's:** the same descriptor form for six channels (`usb_audio_desc.h`; the
streaming descriptors equal SLOOP's but the channel count, the terminal, the endpoint's number and size),
`bcdDevice` 3.22 -> 3.23, `wMaxPacketSize` 640 -> 688 (a 49-frame packet bit-stuffed: 686), `ua_rate_set` /
`ua_control_get` / `ua_control_data` (`usb_audio.c`), the rate back to 44.1 at a bus reset or a new configuration.
The resampler (`usb_audio_stream.c ua_resample`) is SLOOP's table value for value (12 taps, Q15, 1920 B; the
generator's output compares equal to SLOOP's file) run over six channels: `audio.c` calls it after the mix has
staged the block and before the interrupts-off ring copy, so nothing in `fx.c` changed and both Dual Core settings
take the same path. **Measured (host, the C code):** 160 out for 147 in, bit for bit against a reference in any
block sizes; passband flat to 12 kHz, -0.21 dB at 14 kHz, -0.95 at 16, -2.72 at 18; a tone's image (at f + 3900
Hz) 77 dB down or more to 14 kHz, 55.6 dB for 16 kHz, above 20 kHz beyond that (a 20 kHz tone: 23.9 kHz at -14 dB);
1 kHz SNR 80 dB. **The cost** from `build/choralroot.dis` at 205 instructions a us: 236 / 242 instructions an output
frame of six channels, 9 150 a block, **179 us a half + 6 for the longer copies = about 185 us (6.4 %)**, the worst
half 187; at 44.1 kHz about 0.3 us (the test of the rate). Got there in three steps on the unit's compiler: a tap
loop with six 32-bit sums 326 instructions a frame, three channels at a time with 64-bit sums (one multiply-add
instruction) 277 inlined, 236 as functions of their own. **A real cost:** the heaviest case on record is 2580 of
2902 us (CZ-1 chords, both effects); a 48 kHz recording on top is about 2765, where `shed_check` starts giving up
voices. A 16-tap filter would be better at the top (-1.5 dB at 18 kHz, images -80 dB) for about 225 us: not built.
**44.1 kHz bit for bit:** `tests/cr_usbaudio_golden.h` hashes every byte and length of 45 950 packets (three runs,
noise in six channels, an overrun and both nudges in them); the digest was taken with the same file on f402608's
`usb.c` and is compared in `cr_usbaudio_test`. **Counters:** 73 DEBUG is full, so SysEx 79 USB (rate now, rate of
the last whole open, SET_CURs, changes, 48 kHz packets by size, the resampler's us a block); a rate change while
open restarts the open's frame count, so `ua_frames_last_open` is of one rate; `fm1_install.py --debug` and
`uac_check.py` (`--rate`) read it, tested against the simulated FM-1. **Memory:** XIP +4896 B (83.8 %), POOL +724 B
(91.7 %), RAM +128 B (82.2 %). **perf.sh (u)** prints both rates; its 44.1 kHz packet estimate moved from 10 to 19
us with the test file's shape (the host compiler's inlining; the unit's loops are instruction for instruction the
same). **Not tested:** any host (enumeration with two rates, the default rate, SET_CUR's order, 49-frame packets
and 688 bytes on the controller and through a hub, a switch while recording, iOS / Android / Windows), and the cost
on the unit. The test plan is docs/USB-AUDIO.md "The owner's test plan".

## 2026-10-12 (the look-ahead, Sloop 2.6)

3y20. **The owner: the comparison was with Sloop 2.2; its later releases changed the output ("the master limiter looks
0.7 ms ahead"), and Felucca has moved to 1.5.1. Redo it, and give Clean a look-ahead limiter.**

- **Sloop 2.6** (read against the first round's copy of 2.2, built on the host with the same harness, its master's
  shadow exact on 52 renders): the limiter looks one block (32 samples) ahead with a linear gain across the block, a
  35 ms hold and a 46 ms release, a 64-bit product; nothing leaves it over 18000. 153 sounds, the 68 old trims
  unchanged, the new ones levelled the same way (-15 LUFS). Everything else on the way to the DAC word is 2.2's.
  Measured: on the same loud chord its master is 2 to 4 dB cleaner than 2.2's and than Classic's, 5 to 11 dB above
  2 kHz; Clean is 9 to 20 dB under it. The level gap to 0.17: 7.7 dB on a single note (9.4 on the 68 sounds both
  versions have), 4.0 dB on a chord, 13.8 dB on the power-on sound; main's Classic 6.3 / 3.0, Clean 8.8 / 4.9; on the
  loudest chords Classic is level with it and Clean 1 to 2 LU over.
- **What the first round got wrong**: "the limiter and the clip cannot explain the report, Sloop distorts more" was
  true of 2.2 only. Against 2.6, 0.17's master is the rougher one above 2 kHz on loud chords: a candidate for
  "harsh", and what Clean replaces. Level remains the larger measured difference.
- **Felucca 1.0.1 .. 1.5.1** (19 commits, read from GitHub's compare: git could not be aimed at the local checkout
  from the worktree): nothing in the limiter, the clip, the threshold, `OUT_SHIFT` or the levels. Missing here on the
  live path: MASTER read before the audio starts (1.1.5, #137). Dormant: BASS+ x 1.5 (1.4). The list:
  docs/AUDIO-QUALITY.md.
- **Clean's limiter looks ahead** (`fx.c clean_block`): a delay of 32 samples (0.73 ms, 256 bytes of RAM), the gain
  a straight line across each block to what the loudest sample of the block coming out and of the next needs. One
  block ahead and the linear glide are Sloop 2.6's design (credited in `fx.c` and `LICENSING.md`; no code taken);
  the block's peak kept from block to block (no scan of the delay), the hold and release of the first round, the
  exact product and glide are ChoralRoot's. The knee moved to the threshold (26000): nothing the limiter passes
  meets it. A test proves no sample over the threshold for inputs up to 30 dB over, from the first sample.
- **What it does, measured**: the overshoot is gone (the knee's share on a kick 12 dB over: -19 dB before, -89 dB
  now; on loud chords -63 .. -86 dB before, under -90 now; the peak at -2.0 dBFS instead of up to -0.1). **It does
  not lower the loud chords' "dist"** (0.7 to 1.3 dB worse): that figure is the limiter's level steps at each new
  peak of a beating chord, which are the same just before a peak as just after. A longer hold would lower it (200 to
  500 ms: 2 to 5 dB for 0.1 to 0.6 LU): left for the owner, with the files. Loudness unchanged.
- **Length**: 16 to 64 samples measured; 32 chosen (the top stops improving there; one audio block; cheapest). 64 is
  `-DCLN_LA_K=2u`.
- **Cost from the device's listing**: Clean idle 76 instructions a frame (83 before: the envelope is a block's work
  now), limiting about 104 (99). Flash +96 bytes, RAM +256, POOL the same.
- **Not aligned**: the click and the USB recording's part pairs do not pass the limiter and lead Clean's output by
  0.73 ms. Named, not changed.
- **The switch**: one more silent block through the old chain lets the delay play out; both limiters at rest and the
  delay empty at the change.
- **Classic**: all 164 emulator scripts bit for bit against f402608 held at Classic; against 19b6453 the 158 that do
  not play CZ-1 (the other three changed with CZ-1's fix, before this); the 197 goldens unchanged.
- **Seen on the way**: CZ-1 BRASS 1 with the bass, struck again while it sounds, still shows two clicks to
  `wavclicks.py` in Clean (in f402608 as now; not in Classic): in the mix, before the output chain.
- Tools: `tools/master_transient.py`, `master_measure chain`, `tools/master_listen_la.py`, `tools/emu/wavlag.py`,
  `classic_exact.sh REF_CLASSIC=1`. Listening files: `../audio-0.19/lookahead/`. Also: the site's Options list had no
  Output and no Reset Settings. Not run: the browser emulator's test (no emcc); nothing on the device.

also makes it difficult to compare clean and classic, let's make it so the keyboard stays unchanged when entering the
menu and I can still play notes."**

- **Before**: with the Options open the 18 root keys were the menu's: the eleven white ones jumped to entries 0 .. 10
  (Play Style .. Raw Chord Sound), the black ones did nothing, none sounded (`cu_key_press` swallowed them), the
  white roots' LEDs drew that map (the selected entry's key lit). The chord keys and B3 already kept their jobs. The

- **Changing a setting under held notes** (each of the 20 turned up and down under a held chord with the bass, in
  the emulator): no voice dropped, retriggered or left hanging, no click by `wavclicks` anywhere but the strike.
  Output fades over (fx.c). Play Style clears Free's held voices (`cr_set_playstyle`, as ever); the others act on the
  next gesture. On the unit USB Record leaves the bus for a second (the USB side, not the speaker: as ever; not in
  the emulator); Dual Core waits for a restart.

- **Named, not changed**: OCT- / OCT+ close the menu, so the octave cannot be shifted in it; a chord held when

## 2026-10-12 (48 kHz opt-in; GLO and the two sound knobs; the SEQ layer's mock-up)

3y22. **0.19 dev (built; not flashed, nothing run on a host or the unit).** Three decisions of the owner's after the first
48 kHz build was on the unit.

**1. "Make 48 kHz opt-in for now."** The 48 kHz build offered both rates always, on the inference that macOS would
stay at 44.1 kHz (`GET_CUR` 44100, 44100 listed first). It did not: the unit's counters showed `ua_rate 48000` after
one `SET_CUR`, so every recording paid the six-channel resampler (about 185 us a half, 6.4 %), which the heaviest
sounds do not have to spare.

- **Options > USB Rate**, `44.1 kHz` (the default) / `44.1 + 48 kHz`, the entry after USB Level (24 entries now; the
  scripts' entry numbers after it moved up by one). The line under it: "48 kHz: not offered" / "the computer picks
  the rate". Considered and not taken: `USB 48 kHz: Off / On` (reads as "the stream is at 48 kHz" when On, which is
  the computer's choice, not the unit's).
- **Storage**: bit 2 of the settings block's `pick_roots` (`CRS_USB_48K`), as Output took bit 1: no record version
  (15 still), nothing to migrate, every older record reads 44.1 kHz only; automatic (`CRS_PICK_AUTO`); the range of
  the byte is 0 .. 7 now. docs/SETTINGS.md says which of its steps a spare bit takes.
- **Off = 0.18's device**: `ua_off` got a second bit, `UA_OFF_48`; with it `ua_cfg_build` writes the one-rate format
  (11 bytes) and `wMaxPacketSize` 640, 208 bytes in all, and `get_desc` answers `DEV_DESC44`, `bcdDevice` 3.22. The
  bytes are compared with a stored copy of the tag's (`tests/cr_usb018_desc.h`, made by building v0.18's four USB
  files as the host test does and printing `get_desc`). `GET_MAX` 44100; `SET_CUR` is taken and leaves the rate
  (0.18 did exactly that: "UAC1 rounds unsupported values to the closest discrete rate: 44100"; a stall would be the
  other reading of "refused", and would break a host that sets the rate while it opens the stream, which is why
  Melodee kept the request); `ua.r48` is never set, so `audio.c`, which this change does not touch, is never asked
  for 48 kHz. On = the two-rate descriptors of this morning, 3.23, 688.
- **Two `bcdDevice` values**, so a host that cached one state reads the other. 0.18 released with 3.22 for the
  recording and 3.21 for the console (`v0.18:firmware/src/usb.c`); the off state is 3.22 with 0.18's bytes, so a
  unit updated from 0.18 shows the computer nothing new. Nothing in `tools/`, `web/` or `tests/install_test.py`
  names `bcdDevice` (searched for both values again).
- **The replug** is USB Record's (`ua_off_set` / `ua_off_apply`, 0.6 s of rest, `usb_replug`, main.c's
  `USB RECONNECTING`); main.c is untouched, `cu_usb_apply` sends both bits. **USB Record had no guard against a
  transfer** (looked for: `ua_off_apply` checked the time only; the update's own loop, `ota_session`, never returns
  to the main loop, but its handshake and every backup / restore do). Added in `usb.c`: `sysex_byte` stamps
  `usb.sx_ms`, and `ua_off_apply` waits while the last SysEx byte is under 1.5 s old (`UA_REPLUG_QUIET`), then makes
  the change; a frame a host left open does not hold it for ever. A page that polls the unit by SysEx more often
  than every 1.5 s would hold the change until it stops: none of ours does (the installer and the device manager
  talk when asked). With the console presented (USB Record Off, SAFE MODE) USB Rate changes no byte the host is
  given: taken without a replug.
- **Counters**: 79 USB got three values behind its eleven (`ua_48k_offered`, `ua_48k_refused`, `ua_replugs`);
  `fm1_install.py --debug` prints the option as a line; `tools/uac_check.py --rate 48000` stops with "the unit does
  not offer 48 kHz: Options > USB Rate is 44.1 kHz ..." (tested against the simulated FM-1; not run on hardware).
- **Tests**: `tests/cr_usbrate_test.c` (32 checks: both descriptor sets, 0.18's bytes, the console's in both states,
  the requests, the golden digest in both states and under a host that keeps asking for 48 kHz, one replug per
  change, the SysEx guard, no replug with the console presented); `tests/cr_settings_test.c` (the bit, versions 1
  .. 14 read off, automatic); `tests/install_test.py`; `tools/emu/scripts/cr_usb_rate.txt`. The two older USB tests
  run unchanged (their `ua_off = 0` is the option on).
- **Not tested**: any host. In particular that macOS and Windows re-read the descriptors when `bcdDevice` goes 3.23
  -> 3.22 on the owner's Mac (which has cached 3.23 with 48 000 selected); docs/USB-AUDIO.md's plan, part A step 2,
  is that check. Cost: .text +256 B, .bss +16 B.

**2. "If GLO + Algo is changing bass level anywhere, it should change bass engine, let's keep the UI consistent where
we can."** It was not the level anywhere any more: since 0.18 GLO + ALGORITHM was the bass engine on the home pages
and, in a layer or the editor, simply ALGORITHM (the bass sound); but until 0.18 it had been the level in all of
those, so "the shortcut does something else here" was still true of every layer.

- **The rule** (`cr_ui.c cu_epk_ok(part)`, for both knobs): where the knob's plain job is the part's sound, OPT held
  makes it the part's engine picker; where the knob has a job of the place's own, OPT changes nothing about it.
  `cu_pool_home` is gone: the gesture and the read-out's hint line ask the same predicate.
- **GLO + ALGORITHM, before -> after.** Home pages and the Options: bass engine (as 0.18). The layers KEY, PERF, FX,
  BASS, SEQ, LOCK and the view menu, also over the sound editor: bass sound -> **bass engine**. The sound editor's
  pages: bass sound -> **bass engine** (ALGORITHM is not the editor's; its SHIFT is for KNOB 1 .. 4 and SELECT).
  Kept: the PLAY menu (the record mode), the loop editor (Voicing Follow / 0), the editor's picker `L_EDIT` and the
  save dialog (the bass sound, as the knob alone).
- **GLO + PRESETS, before -> after.** Home pages and the Options: chord engine. The same layers: chord sound ->
  **chord engine**. Kept: the sound editor (PRESETS opens its picker, with or without GLO), the PLAY menu (the
  slot), the loop editor (8 steps), the editor's picker (its presets), the save dialog (the chord sound).
- **Why the picker and the save dialog are kept out**, though ALGORITHM's plain job there is the bass sound: each
  holds one sound (a preview that OCT- or HOME takes back; the sound being named and stored). An engine loaded
  under it by a second picker would be previewed, cancelled or saved in its place. This is a judgement, not the
  owner's word: the one-line change is `cu_epk_ok`'s first test. (Named, not changed: on the save dialog PRESETS
  and ALGORITHM alone still change the sounds under the dialog, as they always did.)
- **The read-out** follows: `Hold OPT to change engine` and the engine over the number now show in the layers and,
  for the bass, in the editor; `FM6 · 05/25` where OPT does nothing. No existing check read the old line in a layer.
- **The held-layer rule** is untouched: `cu_shift` is true only when OPT is the button held (`cu.armed`), so with a
  layer's own button held past the hold time the knob is the layer's, GLO pressed with it or not (the script's dump
  31). The bass level stays BASS held + ALGORITHM, tested first in `cu_knob`.
- **Test**: `tools/emu/scripts/cr_glo_knobs.txt` in `test_cr.sh`, 24 checks over 32 dumps: each place above, the
  three kept ones, the picker's and the read-out's screens, the held-layer rule, a bass that is off. The loop
  editor's Follow / 0 is `cr_loop_edit`'s check, unchanged. Docs: MANUAL (Bass, Sounds with the table, the Knobs
  table), PRESETS.md (the before / after table), INTEGRATION, 0.19's notes.

**3. The layers sheet still called the metronome's layer METRO: "Fine to rename."** The button is SEQ since 0.18 and
its hold layer is the metronome.

- `design/make_layers_mockups.py`: state 7's title `7 · METRO · the time signature over the click level` ->
  `7 · SEQ · the metronome: the time signature over the click level`; its description says SEQ held, and what a SEQ
  tap does now (in this layer and with GLO: the click; anywhere else: the loop editor), where it said "METRO tap
  still toggles the click"; the sheet's name and notes say SEQ. The picker's label, **`metronome`**, stays: it is
  what the firmware draws (`cu_picker(.., "metronome", ..)`). The top line's `Metro` is gone from the state
  (`key=""`): the firmware's top line is empty in this layer (the emulator's `cr_metro_row.ppm`), so the mock-up
  had a text the unit never showed.
- Regenerated: `design/choralroot-fm1-layers-mockups.json`, `tests/cr_screens_gen.h` (state 61: its name, its slug
  `61_seq_the_metronome_the_time`, no `.mid`). `tests/gen_cr_screens.py` picks the state by its number, not its
  name: only a comment changed. `sh tests/run_cr_draw.sh`: 92 screens, 0 lint findings, 28 ellipsised (as before).
- **Stale, not re-rendered** (it needs the designer's server): `design/choralroot-fm1-layers-screens.png` (state 7's
  caption and its top line).

**4. Asked the same day: does the six-channel recording have "any negative effect on the audio quality" of the
instrument?** (No behaviour changed; docs/USB-AUDIO.md "Does USB recording affect the sound?" and a test.)

- **By reading**: USB reaches the mix through `ua_stage_on` and `fx_usb_fixed` only. With the stream open the mix
  clears a stage, takes the loop variants with the tap (the same source, two more stores of a saturated half-level
  copy after `mix_l` / `mix_r` are written), copies the master pair out, and feeds the ring: all reads of the mix.
  The "-6 dB and saturated" is the recorded part channels' level. USB Record On with nothing recording is USB Record
  Off as far as the audio path goes. **USB Level is the one that is not a tap**: Fixed puts MASTER behind the
  limiter, with or without a computer; with Output Classic below full MASTER that is a differently limited output
  (by design), with Output Clean a unit of the 24-bit word.
- **By test** (`tests/cr_usbtap_test.c`): one passage, five USB states (Record Off; closed; open, host not reading;
  44.1 kHz; 48 kHz) x Output x USB Level x MASTER full / -18 dB x one core / two x two sound pairs: 32 settings, 160
  renders, the DAC's 24-bit words bit-identical across the five states in every one. The firmware's `fx.c` and
  `usb.c` code runs; the shim's and `audio_block`'s dozen capture lines are copied into the test (they are in
  `choralroot.c` and `audio.c`, which the host unit does not build).
- **The cost, from the listing** (205 instructions a us): the audio ISR 13 100 instructions a half, 64 us, 2.2 %
  (22 us of it in four 5.5 us interrupts-off copies); TIMER5 and the ISR's own service 12 700, 62 us, **and about
  140 SIE register accesses a half whose waits the listing cannot give**: 126 us and those, 4.3 % and up.
  `tools/emu/perf.sh` (u) says 31 us: host instructions, where the copy loops are vector code. The listing is the
  one to believe; the unit has not been measured (`pk_block_us` / `pk_t5_us` with and without a recording). With the
  heaviest patch at 2580 of 2902 us, a 44.1 kHz recording can already bring the shed (85 %) into reach: a voice
  faded, not a distorted sound.
- **Priorities**: USB is polled from TIMER5 (4), above the audio interrupt (3): it preempts the render for its own
  length, which the half's measurement and the shed include; it delays an audio interrupt that falls into TIMER5's
  `usb_poll` by that poll (`pk_lag_us`).
- **`ua_poll_max_us 17860`, `ua_arm_gap_max_us 17698`** (the unit, today): the first times the FM-1's own TIMER5
  cadence and cannot come from a host that does not poll; 17.86 ms without TIMER5 is a stretch with the interrupts
  masked, and the one thing that masks that long is a flash sector erase (documented as 45 ms from the data sheet's
  maximum, never measured: 17.8 ms would be its real time). The second starts again at every open and is either the
  host's first read (about 14 ms: an idle artefact) or the same erase inside an open stream; the two figures are
  too close for chance, so: one erase while the Mac had the stream open. **Inference, not verified**: `irq_gap_us`
  and `irq_gap_pc` in PEAK decide it (about 17 800, an address in `st_erase`'s caller). The driver measures the
  erase (`took`) and nothing reads it.
- **Reported to the DSP side, not changed here** (`fx.c` is another agent's): `mix_loop_u` reloads four constants on
  every pass (it runs out of registers: 38 instructions a frame for `mix_loop`'s 22, where the tap itself is about
  10); the stage is cleared every block although both parts overwrite their channels when they sound.

## 2026-10-12 (CZ-1's level and the click that was left)

3y23. **The owner: "CZ-1 trim sounds good"; and the output round's "CZ-1 BRASS 1 with bass, struck again while sounding,
shows 2 clicks to `wavclicks.py` in Clean".** Two commits on 778a6ad.

- **The trim.** `cz_native.c CZ_VOICE_GAIN` 2: a voice adds `voice_amp(..) * 2`, not `* 4`. The factor is the last
  thing done to the sample, so every sample a voice leaves in its part's sum is exactly half: proven on 33 280 runs
  (a hash of every such sample against a 778a6ad build's halved ones: all the same, none without an exact half) and
  in `cr_cz_test.c` for all 65 tones by kind. The same chord's median: peak +2.4 -> -3.5 dB, RMS -12.3 -> -18.0 dB
  (VA -3.0 / -15.6, FM TONE -3.8 / -16.8, FM6 -4.7 / -20.4); 40 -> 3 of 65 above full scale. `CB_TRIM`'s rows for
  BRASS 1, STRINGS 2, PIPE ORGAN 1 removed (0 % under the limiter without them; none louder than with its trim).
  More than 4 dB from the median and left for the ear: INIT TONE +9.6, STRINGS 1 +5.8, BRASS 1 +5.3, PIPE ORGAN 2
  +4.4; ACCORDION -9.4, SYNTH.LEAD 2 -7.9, JET ROAR -7.1 and five more. User CZ-1 sounds are 6 dB quieter with the
  rest (intended). Left in `cr_settings.c` (another agent's file): the session restores a stored trim.
- **The click.** Reproduced to the millisecond (the chord let go 60 ms and struck again) and followed to the
  sample: no gain or depth step; the flagged window is the first audible one of a re-attack whose DCW the 60 ms
  release left open (third difference 42 against 150 to 215 for the tone's own edges after it). The uPD933 model
  slews an envelope from its current level on every write (MAME `upd933.cpp`), so the engine does what the chip
  does; nothing changed for it. **Found on the way and fixed**: a sounding note struck again at ANOTHER velocity
  stepped the DCA's gain between two samples (43 of 65 tones more than 5 % of full scale; STRINGS 1 a third of its
  level); the matrix had one velocity a run. A line's gain is now kept across blocks and note-ons (`cz_voice_t.ag`):
  bit for bit when the velocity did not change (33 280 runs, 167 emulator WAVs).
- **The 41 jumps at C2** (79 in the larger matrix): each rendered again and set against its wave's definition
  (`tools/cz_pops_why.py`): in all 79 the definition gives the step. The waves' own edges at a DCW depth of 812 to
  1014 of 1023. Left.
- **After both**: 0 wraps, 0 gain steps, 0 holes; the voice into the part peaks at 78 336 (156 672), the dry mix at
  75 787 (151 577); Classic's limiter works in 6.3 % of the runs (54.9 %).
- **Cost** from the device's listing: the trim nothing; the fix +9 instructions a line and block at rest (+0.4 % a
  voice), one DCA curve fewer in a block whose envelope moves, +10 a note-on. XIP -112 B, RAM and POOL the same.
- **Bit for bit**: 161 of 166 emulator WAVs against a 778a6ad build, in Clean and at Classic (the five others play
  CZ-1); 132 goldens unchanged, the 65 CZ-1 ones rewritten (50 of them twice: regress's phrase re-strikes at another
  velocity).
- Tools: `tests/cz_pops.c` (restrike, arp-up, `CZ_TRACE`, `CZ_WHY`, `CZ_VEL2`, `CZ_GAP`, `CZ_CLEAN`, the hash),
  `tools/cz_pops_report.py --jumps / --halves`, `tools/cz_pops_why.py`, `wavclicks.py --both-sides`,
  `cr_cz_brass.txt` in test_cr.sh. Listening files: `../audio-0.19/cz-trim/`. Not run: anything on the device.

## 2026-10-12 (the third round: the hold, the stereo chorus, the click, MASTER at power-on)

3y24. The owner, after hearing the first two rounds of the output chain: dry, little difference between Clean and Classic;
"with very wet FX clean is a definite winner over classic, classic has more crackles and dropouts". His four
decisions: "Chorus improvements sound good" (stereo); a longer limiter hold in Clean, "probably good to do"; the
click lined up with Clean, "ok, do it"; Felucca's fix for MASTER at power-on, "yes". Classic stays in for
comparison, Clean is the default. docs/AUDIO-QUALITY.md "Round 3" has the measurements; docs/FX.md "The chorus,
stereo" the chorus.

- **The hold** (`fx.c clean_block`). Before: the envelope held 40 ms, again by a peak within 0.3 dB; on a loud
  chord it let go between the beats and stepped the level at the next. A plain longer hold (the second round's
  table) keeps the level down after a hit for as long as it is: at 300 ms one hit 12 dB over left a pad 5.7 dB down
  on average for the next second (2.65 at 40 ms) and a kick every half second 2.2 LU quieter. Now: 200 ms
  (`CLN_HOLD`), again within 1.2 dB (`CLN_REHOLD`), and a hit, a peak more than 3.5 dB above the envelope
  (`CLN_HIT`), keeps 40 ms (`CLN_HOLD_HIT`) until the envelope lets go; an envelope left standing above a steady
  sound creeps down to it (`CLN_CREEP`). Measured on the same mixes: WHEEL at velocity 127 -34.7 to -40.5 dB, CZ-1
  at 127 -40.0 to -45.6, very wet WHEEL -35.6 to -46.4, very wet CZ-1 -35.0 to -38.9, sixteenths at 120 BPM over a
  bass -30.4 to -42.4; 0.1 to 0.9 LU on the chords (with the bass still 0.4 to 1.3 LU over Classic), 1 LU on the
  arps; one hit's recovery 466 ms for 455, the kicks 0.2 and 0.3 LU. 300 ms and 500 ms take more and hold a 120 BPM
  kick down for good (1.9, 2.6 LU); a 2.5 dB window takes much more (CZ-1 to -57) for up to 1.7 LU: not taken. The
  release stays 186 ms. Classic untouched.
- **The chorus** (`cr_fx.c xc_block`; in both Output modes). Before: Felucca's one tap to both sides, a comb the
  same in both ears. Now: two taps on the same line, the left one Felucca's own, the right one the LFO a quarter turn
  ahead; the LFO once a block, the read points a line across it; nothing run while nothing is sent. Two taps left
  and right and the LFO a block are SLOOP 2.6's design (credited: `cr_fx.c`, `LICENSING.md`); SLOOP's half turn was
  not taken: as wide, but the mono sum then stands still, where a quarter turn keeps 71 % of the motion. The mono
  sum's deepest notch is 0.18's (-3.8 dB at the default send), reached only where the taps cross. Rate, Depth, the
  send: as they were. A width parameter (`xfp[XP_CWIDTH]`) with no knob: the UI's files were another agent's;
  docs/FX.md has the three lines. Cost: +3 us a half with a chorus send, -29 us with none.
- **Classic's guarantee** changes with it: the output chain is 0.18's bit for bit, and so is any sound without a
  chorus send; with one, the chorus's return differs. `tools/emu/classic_exact.sh` proves both parts: all 0.18
  scripts with the return held at 0.18's (`EMU_CHORUS=mono`: 158 identical, CZ-1's three by CZ-1's fix), and two
  scripts without a chorus send through the stereo build. `CR_CHORUS_MONO` is a test switch; the unit has only the
  stereo return. One check of test_cr.sh read a timbre beside the chorus's comb in the two channels' average
  (`cr_quad_ratiob`, 5.8 % apart on a 5 % bar): it reads the left channel now (`wavpitch.py --left`).
- **The click**: the only thing mixed after the master (metronome and count-in are one generator; no UI sounds). In
  Clean it now starts 32 samples later (`cr_out.c cr_ck_wait`): it is made, not passed through, so that is the
  delay, with no line. **The USB stems**: the CHORD and BASS pairs wait 32 frames in Clean in a line of their own
  (`fx.c ua_parts_late`, 256 bytes of RAM, 9 us a half while recording): the three pairs line up to the sample.
  SCOPE shows what the DAC gets, as before.
- **MASTER at power-on** (`firmware/src/master.c`: Felucca 1.1.5, commit 276f72a, #137). Traced: nothing sounds
  under ChoralRoot's splash (the engine is closed until `cr_out_init`; no chime, no restored chord, no click); the
  window was the half second after it, because `cr_ui_init` set MASTER to 2048 again and the smoothing started at
  mid-scale: a first key or MIDI note at the default's level whatever the knob said. Now the pot is read before
  `audio_init`, followed under the splash, and `cr_out_init` puts its level back after the UI's default; Clean's
  eased MASTER starts on it.
- Size: XIP +564 B, RAM +196 B, POOL unchanged. The stub's bytes unchanged.
- Tests: `tests/cr_output_test.c` (the hold), `tests/cr_fx_test.c` (the chorus), `tests/cr_align_test.c` (new: the
  click, the stems, the mono switch, MASTER at power-on with 0.18's start failing the same check), test_cr.sh
  (`cr_chorus`, `cr_click`, `cr_boot_master`). Tools: `master_rhythm.py`, `master_listen_hold.py`,
  `chorus_response.py`, `chorus_listen.sh`, `tools/emu/wavstat.py`. Listening files: `../audio-0.19/hold/`,
  `../audio-0.19/chorus/`.
- Not run: anything on the unit (the hold and the chorus by ear, the unit's own speaker with the stereo chorus, a
  USB recording's stems in a DAW, the real ADC before the audio starts); the browser emulator's test (no emcc; the
  same sources, its checks do not depend on the chorus's width). Left: docs/USB-AUDIO.md does not say that the part
  pairs are 0.73 ms late with the master pair in Clean.

## 2026-10-12 (the session and a removed trim)

3y25. **0.19 dev (built; not flashed).** The leftover of "CZ-1's level": `cr_settings.c css_part_put` wrote the session
record's `trim` back into `track_t.trim`, so a unit last saved on CZ-1 BRASS 1, STRINGS 2 or PIPE ORGAN 1 (0.18 stored
-10, -6, -12 half-dB steps for them) would have played 5, 3 or 6 dB too quiet under 0.19 until the sound was picked
again. Fixed at the cause: a part's trim belongs to its sound in the running firmware's table, not to the session.

- **The rule** (`cr_settings.h cr_session_part_trim`, a pure function the host test calls with its own tables;
  `css_part_put` hands it `cb_trim`): by the record's pool entry, as `cr_ui.c cu_pool_load` loads a sound by hand: a
  factory preset its `CB_TRIM` row; a user preset bound to a factory preset (an overwritten one) that preset's row;
  an added user preset and INIT none. An edited, unsaved sound the same as its unedited one: no edit ever moved the
  trim (`cu_edited` leaves it). Every path through `css_part_put`: power-on and GLO + SAVE (a backup holds no session;
  Reset Settings loads the pools' sounds).
- **The record is unchanged** (CSS_VERSION, 1024 bytes): `trim` is still written with what the part had, so an older
  firmware reads what it always read, and old records load as before. Seen once after an update: the dump's
  "pending 1" on such a unit (the captured state has trim 0, flash's -10) until the next global save; nothing is
  written by itself.
- **The same class, checked**: `CB_TRIM` is the only per-sound value `cr_bank.c` owns (the curated bank's level rows
  went with docs/PRESETS.md). The record's `p[]` and deep patch are the sound as it was left, by design (the edit
  buffer): a factory preset whose definition an update changes comes back as saved until picked again, and that is
  the session's promise, not a table value. Level, Pan and the sends are the part's. FM TONE's Drive rides in `p[]`
  on purpose (0.18; `test_persist.sh`'s drive runs pass unchanged). CZ-1's -6 dB is in the engine, in no record.
  One inconsistency left as it is, with no row to make it audible today: `cu_engine_switch` sets trim 0 for the
  engine's first preset where `cu_pool_load` would read its row.
- Tests: `tests/cr_settings_test.c t_session_trim` (0.18's record on each of the three sounds, either part: loads,
  trim 0 with 0.19's table and the old value with 0.18's; a sound with a row today gets the row whatever the record
  says; bound and added user presets, INIT, an edited sound, an unknown kind, no sound). `tools/emu/test_persist.sh`:
  BRASS 1 and PIPE ORGAN 1 saved on the two parts, the session's trims set to 0.18's in the flash file
  (`tools/emu/seed_trim.py`), a relaunch and a GLO + SAVE: trim 0 both times (before the fix: -10 / -12 both times);
  the dump prints a `trim:` line. Not run: anything on the device.

## 2026-10-12 (the web installer: what is new, older versions, a guided flow)

3y27. **The owner:** "One thing bothering me on the site is the firmware installer. It does not include release notes
or anything that makes it clear what's new. Would be nice if it showed simple bulleted features for the version and
also let you install older versions." Website work only; no firmware source changed, `fm1ota.js` untouched.
`web/releases.json` (new) lists 0.19, 0.18, 0.17, 0.16 with a date, a tag, the package's sha256 (from each
release's SHA256SUMS; all four releases have one, and the downloaded packages hash to them), 7 or 8 bullets for
players and, for the three older ones, what going back means (from the "Going back" sections of 0.17, 0.18 and
0.19). 0.14 and 0.15 are not offered: no boot stub, and the idle fault. The four releases carry the same stub
(`stub.lock` is version 3, `baee55ed`, at v0.16, v0.17, v0.18 and now), so an install between them is the row
"the same stub, another body" of docs/BOOT-SAFETY.md: no unprotected cut. `web/index_pkg.html` is a flow of six
steps (browser, get ready, test the connection, choose a version, install, done) around the unchanged install; the
chosen package is fetched from the site and checked against the list's sha256 before Install is enabled; a
connection test names what is on the unit; `?demo=N` shows a step without a device and cannot reach MIDI.
`web/make_site.py` publishes every listed version (`--packages`, `--require-all`), `firmware/index.html` and
`SHA256SUMS`, and fills the landing page's "New in X". `.github/workflows/pages.yml` downloads each listed
version's package from its release and fails, naming the version, when one is missing or has another sha256 (the
step was run locally against a stand-in `gh`; the workflow itself was not run). A browser cannot fetch a release
asset: the 302 and the 200 of the download carry no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`. Found on the way: the page's
`.check { display: flex }` showed the "I understand the risk" box although it was `hidden`, since the box
existed; `[hidden] { display: none !important }` now. Tests: `web/test_installer.mjs` (the old checks unchanged
but for the stub DOM, which did not see `h2`), `tests/releases_test.py` (new), `tests/site_test.py` (the
multi-version layout, the refusals, the site as Pages builds it); `tools/fetch_packages.py` (new) fills
`build/scratch/packages/` for a local build. Not run: a real FM-1, Chrome or Edge themselves, Android; going back
on a unit (the notes are the release notes' own). Docs: MANUAL 16, README, BUILDING "Releasing", HANDOFF "Release
steps", DEVICE-MANAGER "The installer beside it".

(e.g. A9 for A7 + 9th rather than A79, Aadd9 for A major triad + 9th which is incorrectly displayed as A9 atm, Asus9
for Asus + 7 + 9 etc...)". **The owner:** "We can add option for the chord names but I would prefer we match the

Orchid is 0.19's naming string for string (`tests/cr_engine_test.c`: 12 roots x 8 qualities x 16 extension sets
against a copy of 6229c8c's `cr_chord_name`). Standard (`cr_engine.c crx_name_std`) is the lead-sheet name of the
notes that sound, derived from `CR_QUALITY` / `CR_EXT_IV`: SUS is the fourth (`sus4`); the 6 is always 9 semitones
(`Cm6`; on DIM the diminished seventh, `Cdim7`); the 7 key always 10 (on DIM half-diminished, `Cm7b5`); the 9
always 14. `Cadd9` / `C9` / `CM9`, `C6/9`, `C13` for 7 + 6 with or without the 9, `C7sus4`, `C9sus4`, `Csus2` for
the power chord with a 9, `C7no3`, `Cm7add4`. Of the 112 chords 84 have a name of their own; the 28 with both
sevenths keep Orchid's (7 `7 M7`, 14 `JAZZ`, 7 `WTF`) and nothing else says `JAZZ` or `WTF`. The major triad stays
`CM`: a single note reads `C`, without its octave, on every screen that names both. docs/CHORD-NAMES.md has the 128
cells with buttons, intervals, both names and the reasons; the test mirrors it and reads every Standard name with an
independent reader whose pitch classes must be `cr_chord_base`'s (allowances: a 13's 9th, the Min + Sus chord's
missing fifth). Display only: `cr_chord_names_set` is one byte outside `cr_t`; every script's WAV is the main
tree's (below). Stored in bit 3 of the block's `pick_roots` (`CRS_NAMES_STD`), no record version, automatic; an
older record reads Orchid, and 0.19 and older take their default for the byte when it is 8 .. 15 (Output Clean, USB
Rate 44.1 kHz until set there). No glyph added: the qualities (`M m dim sus4 sus2 +`) are in the 37-character
104 px face, the rest is the superscript's, the 40 px ASCII face (`/` included). **Found on the way, both in Orchid
mode too:** the screen's copy of the superscript was 8 bytes against the engine's 12, so the views showed
`Cm add4 JAZZ` as `add4 JA` (seen in main's emulator: `name 'G#|m|add4 JA'`): 12 now, asserted against
`CR_SUP_MAX`; and with it whole the longest names ran off Geek Out's left edge (the name is centred on x 70 with

playing name's 78 px): 0 lint findings, nothing cut; Standard's widest own name is `G#madd9add4`, squeezed to 58 %
at 104 px (Orchid's `G#m add4 JAZZ`: 56 %), 105 px on the looper's line (110), and none of Standard's own names
needs the playing name's 10 px face. Size: .text +880 B, RAM +16 B (81316, 82.7 %), POOL +8 B (315496, 91.7 %). The
Options entry moved every later entry by one: 16 emulator scripts and the entry numbers in `test_cr.sh` and
`test_persist.sh`.

## 2026-10-12 (the 6th above a seventh: Options > 6th with 7th)

3y30. **The same user:** "it would be nice if the 6th was played an octave higher if either 7th is enabled
(effectively making it a 13th chord)". **The owner:** "Let's add as option you can turn on, defaulted off."
**Options > 6th with 7th: In place / Octave up**, after Secret Chords (entry 3 of 26), In place the default. The
name was chosen for the row and for a player without the manual: the entry says which notes it is about, the value
what happens to the 6th (`13th Voicing: Off / On` would need the manual to say what Off is); the line under it reads
`6 + 7: the 6th under the 7th` / `6 + 7: the 6th on top, a 13th`. **Octave up**: a chord with the 6 and at least one
seventh (m7, M7 or both) has its 6th 21 semitones above the root instead of 9, before the voicing arranges it
(`cr_engine.c cr_chord_base_ex`, the one place a chord's notes come from; `cr_chord_base` is main's function with
the option off). Decisions: every chord type alike, the secret ones and DIM too (on DIM + 7 the 9-semitone note is the
13th of a half-diminished chord, as Standard already names it, `Cm13b5`; one rule reads and sounds the same
everywhere); both sevenths at once: raised; the 13th is above the 9th (14) and is the chord's top note, so KNOB 1
rotates it as it rotates the 9th (one step down gives In place's notes at 0); chords without a seventh (`C6`,
`C6 9`), single notes and the bass: untouched; Key Mode: the extensions were never corrected to the scale and are
not now; where root + 21 is above MIDI 127 the 6th stays in place rather than be dropped (the same number of notes
as In place for every root: no extra voice, none lost). **Everything that shows or sends a chord's notes reads the
engine's**: the sound, MIDI out, the Raw Chord stream, the arp, strum and patterns, the key LEDs, the views' note
lists, the emulator's dump (`cr_chord_base` had no caller outside `cr_engine.c`). **The looper** stores root, type
and extension bits, so a recorded chord follows the setting at the moment each step is struck, as it follows the
live voicing, Extension Addition and the Perform mode; a step's own voicing rotates the raised note the same way.
**A change acts from the next chord on** (a press, a pad, a loop step, a chord key changing a held chord); a chord
that sounds keeps its notes and its note-offs are its own: tested with the option turned under a held chord, a
latched one, the Raw stream and the bass, a running strum, arp and pattern, and a sounding loop voice (no stuck
note, no double note-on, no stray note-off, no event left). **Names**: unchanged in both namings (`C7 6`; `C13`).
**Stored** in bit 4 of the block's `pick_roots` (`CRS_SIXTH_UP`), no record version, automatic; the engine's
(`cr_t.sixth_up`, in a byte that was padding: `cr_t` did not grow; `cr_settings_apply` / `cr_settings_capture`,
the event `CRE_SIXTH`). Read before taking the bit: `crs_sanitize` is `CRS_FIX(pick_roots, 0, hi)` with `hi` 1 at
v0.17 / v0.18 and 7 at v0.19 (`git show v0.19:firmware/src/cr_settings.c`), 15 on main; a value above it gives
that firmware its default for the whole byte. So 0.19 with Octave up set reads Output Clean, USB Rate 44.1 kHz and
Chord Names Orchid until they are set there; with In place set it reads the byte as before. The same price the
three earlier bits paid, and a version bump would not change what an older firmware does (docs/SETTINGS.md).
**In place is main's, bit for bit**: the golden renders 197 with 0 changed; `tests/cr_engine_test.c`: 8 types x 16
extension sets x 28 roots against a copy of main's builder, each voiced at 13 KNOB 1 positions, and 5600 chords each
way through a loop voice, a loop step with its own voicing, a pad and the keys; every emulator script through this
tree's emulator and one built from `git archive e51baa5`, each on its own copy of the script: 179 of 179 WAVs
identical byte for byte (`cr_opt_keys` walks one more entry and waits 120 ms less after it, so its WAV is main's
too). Octave up changes only chords with the 6 and a seventh: the unit test's cells, and A + 9 on a flash that says
Octave up is a new unit's WAV. Note for the next reader: most of `tools/emu/perf.sh`'s scenarios hold MAJ + 6 + M7
+ 9, so their notes (not their cost) differ with the option on. **Cost**: note selection when a chord is built,
nothing per sample, `.ram_text` unchanged; `.text` +368 B (468064), RAM +0 (81316, 82.7 %), POOL +0 (315496,
91.7 %). The Options entry moved every entry from Velocity on by one: 21 emulator scripts, `cr_opt_keys`' walk, and
the entry numbers in `test_cr.sh` and `test_persist.sh`; `cr_layers.txt` turned KNOB 1 on entry 3 (Velocity) and
turns it on entry 4 now. New: `tools/emu/scripts/cr_sixth.txt` (14 dumps: both values, the chord held through the
change, M7, no seventh, with the 9, KNOB 1, the key LEDs), `cr_sixth_set.txt` / `cr_sixth_check.txt` (saved by
itself, back after a relaunch); a draw check of the row (0 lint findings, nothing cut). The mock-up generator's
Options sheet (`design/make_mockups.py`, state 12) is the first design's list and has none of the entries added
since: not touched, as stale as it was. **Listening files** (the emulator, not the unit):
`../audio-0.20/sixth/`: C7 + 6, CM7 + 6, Cm7 + 6, C7 + 6 + 9 on FM6 TINE EP and VA LUSH PAD, each In place then
Octave up, at Voicing 0, +2 and -2, with the notes of every strike in the README. Not run: the unit (nothing was
flashed; the owner has not heard it), `tools/emu/classic_exact.sh` (nothing near the audio path changed; it needs
the 0.18 reference build), the old firmwares themselves (their rule for the byte was read in their source and
modelled in `tests/cr_settings_test.c`).

## 2026-10-12 (the chorus: no clicks under a turning Depth, the Width knob; 0.21 begins)

3y31. **The owner:** "This FX pops and crackles when adjusting the depth which I don't like." and "Since it's stereo
now, and we have Knob 3 as empty slot we should be able to add a parameter for Stereo Width." **0.21 dev (built; not
flashed).** `FELUCCA_VERSION` "ChoralRoot 0.21", `docs/releases/0.21.md` begun.
**The cause, measured before the fix** (`tests/cr_fx_test.c` "chorus glide", which keeps 0.20's block word for word
beside the new one; the read point is taken from the output: the send is a ramp, so a tap's output is the time it
reads at): `cr_fx.c xc_block` drew each tap's read line from the LFO's point at the block's start to its point at the
end with that block's Depth and Width, so a block began where the last one ended only while neither moved. Depth 60
-> 61 with the LFO at its top: the read point steps 6.0 samples at the block's edge (a 110 Hz sine's second
difference 1862 for a steady 12); Width 60 -> 61 at Depth 127: 3.3 samples (212); the FX layer's detent is 2 units,
so twice that a click. Rate was never a cause: it is the LFO's increment, the phase runs on (swept and thrown, the
new block's samples are 0.20's).
**The fix**, in `xc_block` alone: (1) each tap's last read point is state (`xc.ra`, `xc.rb`, Q8) and the next
block's line starts there; (2) the depth (`xc.dc`, 16ths of the old unit) and the right tap's lead (`xc.oc`, phase)
go to what is set by a 64th of what is left a block, rounded away from zero: a one-pole of 64 blocks, 46 ms
(`XC_GL` 6), that lands exactly. (1) alone would turn the click into a 6-sample slide inside 32 samples, a 19 %
pitch blip. After: Depth 60 -> 61: 0.06 samples a sample, second difference 12, the steady figures; a sweep by hand
0.13 / 12; Width a detent 0.13 / 12, a sweep 0.19 / 12; the whole range at once (no knob does it) 0.31 / 76 for
Depth, 0.38 / 82 for Width, where 0.20's block stepped 561 and 516 samples. 46 ms chosen against 23 and 93: the
three measure alike for a detent and a sweep by hand and differ only for the range at once (0.56 / 0.31 / 0.19
samples a sample); 93 ms lags the hand twice as long and needs 1.6 s to land the Width.
**At rest it is 0.20's block to the bit**: the glided depth is 16 x the old one and `(s x 16 d) >> 12` is
`(s x d) >> 8`; the stored point is the one the old block computed again. The test: noise and a sine, 2 s, six
settings of Rate / Depth / Width, blocks of 32 and of 32 / 8 / 24 / 16 in turn; a second after a move the samples
are 0.20's again (the glide lands); both knobs turned while the line is empty, then a send: 0.20's samples from the
first one (an empty line glides nothing: power-on, a loaded session and a note after a knob turned in silence start
in place). The golden renders: 197, 0 changed.
**Width**: KNOB 3 of the Chorus page (`cr_ui.c CU_FXP`, the glyph `CR_G_WIDTH`), `xfp[XP_CWIDTH]` 0 .. 127, default
127 (0.19's and 0.20's chorus; 0: both ears the left tap), read as a number as the reverb's Width, 2 a detent as
every 0 .. 127 cell of the layer, glided as Depth. Kept in the session under a new tag, `CSS_FXP_XC_WIDTH` 30
(`CSS_FXG`, the static assert, docs/SETTINGS.md): **23 of the FX block's 24 parameter entries are used now, one is
left**. Saved data, read in `git show v0.19:` and `v0.20:firmware/src/cr_settings.c` (the same code: `v0.20` is
`f9db021`) and run through an emulator built from `git archive f9db021`: (a) a session written by 0.20 has no tag 30 and
loads with Width 127 (run: `30=127`, everything else as 0.20 wrote it); (b) 0.20 reading a session with tag 30
applies everything else and never looks the tag up (run: its 22 parameters as stored, 23 entries being inside its
24; nothing reset), its chorus is its fixed Width 127; with no save under 0.20 the record still holds `30=123` on
the way back, and after SAVE held under 0.20 (its record is built from its own 22 tags) 0.21 reads 127. 0.19: the
same loader and writer by its source, not run. The session is not in a
backup (`cr_backup.c`: a restore of the settings empties it), so the backup format is untouched.
**Cost** (the device listing, 205 instructions a microsecond): a running block 1722 instructions for 1778 (51 a
sample for 52; about 90 a block for 114: two sines for four, the glides a compare each at rest), about 106 a block
while a glide runs, +10 in the one block after rest, an idle block 161 for 156. `.text` +144 B (468208; `xc_block`
632 B for 550), RAM +16 B (81332, 82.7 %), POOL +0 (315496, 91.7 %), `.ram_text` unchanged.
**Tests and fixtures touched**: `tests/cr_fx_test.c` (+9 checks, 58), `tools/emu/scripts/cr_fx_row.txt` and
`test_cr.sh` (the Chorus page's cells read `Rate Depth Width Send`, KNOB 3 traces `width 125`),
`tools/emu/scripts/session_set.txt` and `test_persist.sh` (Width turned to 123, saved, back after a relaunch as
`30=123`), `design/make_fx_mockups.py` state 4 and its JSON (the Chorus row with its Width cell; the designer's PNG
is as stale as it was for the glyphs). **Listening files** (the host test's renderer, `cr_fx_test --chorus-listen`
through `tools/chorus_glide_listen.sh`: the firmware's block and 0.20's beside it, a held chord at send 105; not
the emulator's whole path, not the unit): `../audio-0.21/chorus/`: Depth in detents before and after, Width 127 ->
0 -> 127 (and the same without the glide), Width 0 / 64 / 127. **Run** (logs in `build/scratch/logs/`): `./build.sh` (stub version 3, sha256
baee55ed142fe1cc), `run_cr_tests.sh` (cr_fx_test 58 checks ok), `run_cr_draw.sh` (92 screens, 0 lint findings),
`tools/emu/test_cr.sh` (891 ok, PASS), `test_persist.sh` (PASS), `test_retired.sh` (PASS), `run_tests.sh` (ALL HOST
TESTS PASSED), `install_test.py`, `releases_test.py` (ALL OK; it notes that 0.21 is not in `web/releases.json` and
need not be yet), the five `web/test_*.mjs`, `CR_VERSION=0.21 build_web.sh` + `test_web_emu.mjs` (PASS). Not run: the unit (nothing flashed; the
owner has not heard it), `tools/emu/classic_exact.sh` (it needs the 0.18 reference build; the script it compares
has no knob turning).
