## 2026-10-17 (Bubba Machine: back to V15, and the installers tell it apart)

3y176. **An FM-1 on Bubba Machine 0.8.0-beta back to the official V15, and both installers recognise Bubba Machine (the
owner, 2026-10-17: "I flashed this firmware https://github.com/Erbubar23/bubba-machine and it softbricks the FM-1 with
no way to go back to any other firmware", then "Please try and get my device back to V15").** Before:
`tools/fm1_install.py --info` read `FM-1_003 [running] port: FM-1 Bubba Machine`, no unit ID, and called it "the
official M-VAVE firmware (FM-1_003)": Bubba Machine answers the handshake as FM-1 version 3 (its `FM1_IDENT`, so that
M-UPGRADE offers V15) and its USB product name is "FM-1 Bubba Machine". **Why nothing else installs over it**: its
updater (`hal/fm1/fm1_update.c` there) speaks M-VAVE's protocol but takes only a package whose update loader is M-VAVE's
own (`usb_hid_ota.bin`: header CRC 0xEBAA, data CRC 0x5881, 0x4DE1 bytes, load address 0x01C0A800) and whose `flash.bin`
head 0x0000..0x3FFF equals the unit's; every Felucca-family package (ChoralRoot's included) carries the Felucca loader,
so Bubba refuses it after the first reads, erases its staging area and writes nothing. **Back to V15**: `caffeinate
-dimsu python3 tools/fm1_install.py MvaveFM1Unbricker/FM-1.fwsc --yes` (M-VAVE's file, sha256
db1642b2b6fa5c2cccb11ffd13878068bb28601678d3644049f99dc40e7edb8a): 74 reads (the header, the loader, the head),
"switching to update mode", M-VAVE's loader wrote it, "done: the FM-1 runs FM-1_015", exit 0; `--info` after: `FM-1_015
[running] port: FM-1`. No backup was possible (Bubba Machine has no backup protocol). Which physical unit it was cannot
be told from here (no unit ID on Bubba or on stock). Bubba's own data stays in the settings area: V15 may read it as
garbled presets (System > Reset Patches; MvaveFM1Unbricker `repair-settings`). **The installers**:
`classify_firmware(identity, info, port)` and `classifyFirmware(identity, info, port)` (web/fm1ota.js) take the MIDI
port's name; a port named "...Bubba..." on an FM-1 identity is kind `bubba`, "Bubba Machine (FM-1_003)", refused, also
with `--force` and with the page's override (the override box is not offered: Bubba's updater would refuse the package
anyway), and the text says the way out: the official V15 with this tool, the page's "Return to official V15" or Bubba's
own installer, then ChoralRoot. `--info` names it and prints the same. FM-1_003 on the FM-1's own port is still the
stock firmware. The return to V15 stays allowed over anything. docs/INSTALL-COMPAT.md: the policy's row and a "Bubba
Machine" section; README's "Over which firmware". **Tests**: tests/install_test.py (seven port cases; installs over
Bubba with and without `--force` refused, exit 8, no upgrade sent; FM-1_003 on the port "FM-1" installed; `--info`; the
port cases against fm1ota.js); web/test_installer.mjs (`FakeFM1` takes a port name; the page over Bubba refuses with the
text and no override box, the override ticked still refuses, "What is on my FM-1?" names it; FM-1_003 on "FM-1"
installs). `CR_VERSION=0.25.1 sh tests/run_all.sh` ALL SUITES PASSED (23 suites). Not pushed.

session's permission rules (the flash-025 session installed A and B, below). `engine_t.split` (core.h): a voice's weight in 10 us, P5 44 (`P5_VOICE_US` 440), the VA 28
(`VA_VOICE_US` 280, the unit's row / 8), 0 for the rest; `fx.c mix_parts_split` splits the chord part when its engine
has it (was `P5_IS(t0)`). The protocol is 3y46's, unchanged: core 0 prepares every voice before the ONE hand-over
(`voice.c track_render_x`, the `p5x` list), `c1_part1_job` renders the bass part and then core 1's share, now through
the engine's `render2` / `render`, core 0 renders the rest, waits, adds; core 1 not answering, refusing or given up:
core 0 runs the same job itself (DUALCORE.md "Reliability", "The persistent fallback" as they were). Melodee's
streaming queue is not taken. **The side**: the VA's `render2` (SPREAD, USPREAD) on core 1 adds into `p5x_side` (128 B
of POOL) and sets `p5x_side_on`; core 0 adds it into the part's side after the wait as it adds `p5x_buf` into the mid
(`p5x_run` takes `side`, `side_on`). **The weights** (`p5x_assign(t0, ..)`): a chord voice its engine's weight, the
bass as before (P5 voices 440, another engine 75 a unit), core 0's side x 1.07 (`P5X_C0_PCT`, TIMER5's ticks nested in
core 0's audio ISR: Melodee's "CPU0 service allowance", the idea), the four a core and `P5_FILL_C1_FIRST` P5's alone.
VA over a sounding MONO bass (core 0 + core 1): 8 voices 4 + 4, 7 4 + 3, 6 3 + 3, 5 3 + 2; over a P5 stack of four 7 +
1. For P5 the factor changes no split with a MONO or a P5 bass; it moves one voice to core 1 for a 2-, 4- or 6-voice
chord over a POLY bass of another engine of three voices (or six, seven of one unit): the samples are the same. P5's
78 PEAK counts stay P5's (a VA chord over a P5 bass counts the bass, as `p5x_bass_count` did). **The audit**
(docs/VA.md "Two cores", as PROPHET.md's for P5): `va_render` writes its own `va_vs[part][voice]` and `voice_t` (the
noise seeds are per voice, `s[2]`; BROWN / VINYL's state in `nz`), the buffers it is given, and from the latest
note's voice alone `va_pan_off` / `va_rate_off` (one writer, read on core 0 after the join); it reads `va_lfo`
(`va_block`'s, before the hand-over) and the patch; its callees are pure. Core 1's stack: `c1_part1_job` 68 B ->
`va_render` 844 B, shallower than a VA bass's path through `track_render_x` (212 B). **`CR_RAM_VA=1` did not link**
(since GRAIN shares `va_fweights`, 3y118, it is called out of line: "relocation truncated to fit"): a veneer in
`cr_ram.c`; `ram_code.py cmp` 4 moved, 0 functions differ, veneers `memset` and `va_fweights`, RAMTEXT 16 120 of 24 576.
Size (default): XIP 543 244 B (+416, 94.7 %), RAM 80 544 (+0), POOL 308 636 (+128). **Tests**: `tests/cr_c1_thread_test.c`
19 pairs (+4: VA chord over FM6, CZ-1, MORPH PAD over PUNCH BASS with core 1's SPREAD side in 1463 of 3000 blocks, a
UNISON chord with USPREAD); for the six VA-chord pairs inline = thread = unsplit = all to core 1 = none, every sample,
each core rendering chord voices (at most four each over a VA, FM6 or CZ-1 bass; seven on core 0 over a P5 stack); ThreadSanitizer 0 reports over 19 pairs, the planted word still
reported. test_cr.sh `cr_va_eight` (new script: MORPH PAD, eight MIDI notes, PUNCH BASS, a chord change): eight voices
and the bass, inline = `EMU_C1=thread` bit for bit, `EMU_C1=nostart` the chord seven under the bass (one core's
budget), `EMU_C1=hang` one core's WAV for the first 7.9 s and Dual Core Off; the emulator's stages (device us a half,

for bit. `tools/perf_unit.py`: a third table, "cores" (78 PEAK's `pk_c0_busy_us`, `pk_c1_busy_us`, `pk_c0_wait_us`,
`pk_fx_join_us`, `pk_c1_idle_us`). **Checked**: `sh tests/run_all.sh` 23 suites, every one PASS but test_cr's new hang
check on the first run (its `c1 gave up` is printed with EMU_STAGES only: set), then test_cr.sh alone 1092 ok, 0 FAIL;

## 2026-10-17 (FM6's chord voices on both cores)

3y187. **FM6's chord voices on both cores, as the VA's (3y177) and P5's (3y46)** (the orchestrator's go, after B's
readings; numbered after main's 3y186 so that nothing is renumbered at the merge). `ENG_FM6.split` 20 (`FM6_VOICE_US`
200: TINE EP 8 notes, part0 1524 .. 1627 on the unit under B). **The audit** (docs/FM6.md "Two cores") found two
things a voice's render shared. (1) **The render's scratch** (`fm6_scr`: the modulator buses and the voice's sum) was
chosen by part (`CR_C1_IDX`: part 1 on core 1), so core 1's chord voices would have used core 0's. It is now chosen by
the core that runs (`CR_C1_SELF()`: the `cnum` register on the device, `hal/fm1_cpu1.h fm1_cnum`; a thread-local on
the host's job thread; 3y45's part test kept for the inline stub). Core 1 renders the bass part and then its chord
voices one after the other, so its one scratch serves both: **no third `fm6_scr`** (the review's 384 B). (2) **The
part's AMS shares** (`P->pt.amd`, `pok`, `pt[]`) were figured lazily in `fm6_control` by the first voice of a block to
need one. Every voice of a block has the part's one modulation (`fm6_amd`, split out: the LFO, its delay, the
controllers, the EG bias), so `fm6_block` now figures the shares on core 0 before the hand-over, for the operators
that sound and when a voice is active (`fm6_ams_share`, the old code moved; both `always_inline`, so `fm6_control`
compiles as it did); the voices then only read them. The same values, so the same samples. **Proof that both were
needed**: ThreadSanitizer with the scratch by part: 10 reports (`fm6_op`'s buses); with the shares lazy: 4
(`fm6_ams_share`); with both changes: 0 over 22 pairs. `CR_RAM_FM6=1` linked no more (`fm6_block` in flash now calls
`fm6_mulhi` in RAM through the inlined share): a veneer in `cr_ram.c`; with `CR_RAM_VA` beside it RAMTEXT 22 372 of 24
576, `ram_code.py cmp` 0 functions differ. **Cost**: RAM +0, POOL +0, XIP +944 B (543 252 -> 544 196 of 573 440, 94.9 %;
RAMTEXT 16 120 as B). Single-core package: XIP 541 200 (94.4 %), RAM 77 420. SMK-37: XIP 542 924 (94.7 %), RAMTEXT 16
260, RAM 81 340. **Tests**: `tests/cr_c1_thread_test.c` 22 pairs (+3: FM6 chord over CZ-1, TINE EP over DEEP SUB, a
patch with AMS 1..3 on every operator and the LFO's AMD 90, whose shares were in use in 2948 of 3000 blocks); for
the six FM6-chord pairs inline = thread = unsplit = all to core 1 = none, every sample, each core rendering chord voices (four and four over an FM6, VA
or CZ-1 bass; over a P5 stack of four, core 1 up to four and core 0 all eight in its blocks).
test_cr.sh `cr_fm6_eight` (new: TINE EP 8 MIDI notes, DEEP SUB, a chord change): eight voices and the bass,
inline = `EMU_C1=thread` = `EMU_C1=nostart` (eight FM6 voices and the bass fit one core's budget) bit for bit, the
WAV 765c033's; `EMU_C1=hang` one core's WAV for the first 6.4 s, Dual Core Off; test_cr 1098 ok. **Bit-exact, one core
and two**: the 317 goldens (one core) unchanged; built with `CR_CPU1=1` and core 1 alive
(`build/scratch/va-split/goldens_c1.sh`), split = unsplit for all 317, and that set = 765c033's = 13b0adb's.
**The emulator** (`cr_fm6_eight`, device us a half, avg / max): part0 249 / 684 and part1 28 / 113 before (765c033),
146 / 578 and 145 / 418 after (a second run 146 / 461, 145 / 392). **The estimate for the unit** (not measured): under B
TINE EP over DEEP SUB is block 2468 dry / 2565 recording, part0 1524 / 1627, part1 249 / 257, no late half and no shed.
Four voices a core would take the longer side to about 950 .. 1000; the emulator's peaks fall less than its averages

## 2026-10-19 (signed releases: The Firmware Hub's shared build workflow)

3y190. **Releases built and signed by The Firmware Hub's shared workflow (the owner: "thefirmwarehub now supports signing
see https://thefirmwarehub.com/kb/#sign we should get this working with ChoralRoot").** The hub's knowledge base ("How to
sign your release", "What the hub checks", "Built from source", "Getting your firmware listed", 2026-10-19): a card
gets **Built from source on GitHub** when the package on the release has a GitHub artifact attestation signed by
`zvenson/fm1-build/.github/workflows/build.yml` at `refs/tags/v1` (only that ref is trusted), from the project's own
repository, for a release tag; the hub checks the signature, Sigstore's certificate and log, and that the attested
SHA-256 is the file it serves, so a file rebuilt or uploaded by hand afterwards loses it. Its sync runs every three
hours; a release that fails a check is not offered and the last good one stays. **The workflow** (v1 = commit
`85a003ab455b1c074b0bd6d7c282c096a464b973`, 2026-10-18, "Tested with Shine On"; Melodee and PIGEON are signed by it,
the catalogue's `provenance` says so): `workflow_call` with the inputs `build` (a bash command, `eval`ed in the
repository root), `packages` (a glob), `test` (optional), `tests-block` (true) and `sdk` (true: a clone of
`AC79NN_SDK_V1.2.1_2023-12-13` from gitee to `~/fw-AC79_AIoT_SDK`, `$AC79_SDK`); one job on `ubuntu-24.04`, every
action pinned to a SHA: checkout v4.2.2, setup-python v5.6.0 with Python 3.12, `pip install Pillow` (unpinned, no
fontTools), JieLi's toolchain from `https://pkgman.jieliapp.com/s/linux-toolchain` (as `tools/get_toolchain.sh`, no
cache; `$JIELI_TOOLCHAIN`), our command natively (no Docker: build.sh and tools/build.py run the toolchain natively on
Linux x86-64), `sha256sum` of the glob into its own `SHA256SUMS`, attest-build-provenance v2.4.0 over the glob,
upload-artifact v4.6.2 (`packages`: the glob and `SHA256SUMS`), and on a tag softprops/action-gh-release v2.3.2 with
the glob and `SHA256SUMS` and `generate_release_notes`. No name, body, draft or prerelease input: it creates a plain
release, and on an existing one (a draft too: it finds the tag among all releases) it keeps the name, body, draft and
pre-release flags and replaces only same-named assets. Any number of files: the glob decides. It has no outputs, but
jobs after it in the calling workflow run (`needs:`) and can take its artifact. **Reproducible**: fm1-build differs
from our CI in Python (3.12, not 3.11), Pillow (the newest, 13.0.0 today, not 11.1.0), fontTools (none, not 4.66.1),
Raqm (no libfribidi) and the SDK (sdk: false keeps our vendored `tools/sdk`). Rebuilt v0.25.1 in `ubuntu:24.04`
(linux/amd64, Docker on the Mac, the toolchain `20250324.1`): Python 3.12.3 with Pillow 11.1.0, fontTools 4.66.1 and
Raqm gives **the release's four sha256s** (ef57eacc.., 1dcac28c.., 052a08af.., 2e4936ca..: the Python version makes no
difference to ChoralRoot's packages, which hold no samples); Pillow 13.0.0 with fontTools 4.67.0 gives other packages
(2aa692af.., 3512ce8b..); without libfribidi `gen_aa_font.py` fails (Raqm requested and missing). The toolchain:
JieLi's link serves `jieli-linux-toolchains-20260730.1` (Last-Modified 2026-10-09) while the Mac has `20250324.1`;
our CI's v0.25.1 run fetched it fresh (its cache entry was created in that run, the cache is per tag) and its packages
are the sums the Mac built and `web/releases.json` lists, so the newer download makes the same bytes. **The change**:
`tools/hub_build.sh` is the build command (also runs on the Mac: `tools/hub_build.sh X.Y`): on GitHub's runner it
installs libfribidi0, libraqm0 and zip and pip-installs Pillow 11.1.0 and fontTools 4.66.1 over the workflow's Pillow,
refuses other versions and a Pillow without Raqm, runs `./build.sh --release` (the three SMK models for X.Y-smk37),
checks the files and `SHA256SUMS` as release.yml did, checks the packages' sha256s against `web/releases.json` (both;
on a tag of a site-publishing version an unlisted version is an error) or `web/releases-smk37.json` (six, when listed),
so a package that is not the pinned one is never signed, and copies the packages and apps to the repository root
(`choralroot-*`: the workflow's `SHA256SUMS` then names them as ours does, four lines, or twelve). `--version` prints
the version and board (from the argument, the tag, or a dispatched run's input read from the event file, never
interpolated into a command), `--check DIR` checks a folder. `.github/workflows/release.yml`: **draft** (the release as
a draft with the notes, now with the `gh attestation verify` line, `LICENSE`, `LICENSING.md`, `LICENSES.zip`, and
`SMK37.md` for the SMK; it stops first when a site-publishing FM-1 version is not `web/releases.json`'s first entry or
the release is already published: a public release's signed files are never rebuilt), **build** (`uses:
zvenson/fm1-build/.github/workflows/build.yml@v1`, `build: bash tools/hub_build.sh`, `packages: choralroot-*`, `sdk:
false`; contents, id-token and attestations write; it also runs alone from the Actions tab: signed, no release, the
artifact `packages`), **publish** (downloads the draft's `choralroot-*` and `SHA256SUMS`, counts four or twelve,
`sha256sum -c`, `gh attestation verify` each with `--signer-workflow`, `--signer-digest` v1's commit (`FM1_BUILD_V1_SHA`:
a moved v1 tag stops the release), `--source-ref`, `--source-digest` and `--deny-self-hosted-runners`, `hub_build.sh
--check`, then `gh release edit --draft=false`, and pages.yml as before, for the FM-1 and the SMK). Gone: our own
toolchain cache, setup-python and the SDK check step (tools/build.py checks the three files' SHA-256 itself), and
`ATTRIBUTION.txt` (only a FELUCCA_SAMPLE build has it: hub_build.sh stops on one). The flow keeps the order the
installer needs: the sums are committed before the tag from the owner's build, and the signed packages are checked
against them before the release is public, so what the hub compares (the attested SHA-256 and the file it mirrors) and
what the installer compares (`web/releases.json`) are the same bytes. BUILDING.md "Releasing" (the three jobs, the
fallback without the toolchain now leaves an unsigned release), "Signed builds" and "Checking a package"; HANDOFF's
release steps. **The identity**: the hub's live table (catalog.json, 2026-10-20 02:03 UTC; 74 firmwares with an
identity) has FM-1_920 as ChoralRoot's alone ("own"); shared are FM-1_900 (16 firmwares), 910 (3), 911, 950, 951 and
700 (2 each). So ChoralRoot keeps FM-1_920: a version-encoded identity in X0X's style (FM-1_9200026) would gain
nothing on the hub and break what keys on three digits: the hub resumes an interrupted Felucca-loader update only for
FM-1_9NN, our installer's `OUR_LOADER` (`web/fm1ota.js`, `_[789]\d\d`) and `web/make_site.py`'s `FM-1_9\d\d` would
refuse it, and the SMK identities (SMK-37 Pro_920 ...) and every unit updating from an older version have 920. Seen in
the catalogue: the hub serves ChoralRoot's **single-core** package (`fw/choralroot-0.25.1.fwsc`, sha256 1dcac28c..,
`sha256_1core`), not `choralroot-0.25.1.fwsc` (the KB: "If your release has more than one .fwsc, tell us which one
is the firmware"): for the owner to tell the hub. **Checked**: actionlint 1.7.7 (release.yml, pages.yml: no errors),
shellcheck 0.10.0 on hub_build.sh (clean); `--version` and `--check` on the cases (0.25.1 matches, the 13.0 build is
refused, an unlisted 0.27 is an error on a tag and a notice otherwise, -rc a notice, `bad..` refused); the workflow's
steps around the command simulated in `ubuntu:24.04` (Python 3.12.3 venv, `pip install Pillow`, the toolchain, `bash
tools/hub_build.sh` as a v0.25.1 tag build): the four sums are the published v0.25.1 `SHA256SUMS` exactly; and this tree (13b0adb with
hub_build.sh) as a run from the Actions tab, version 0.26-hubtest: its four sums are the Mac's `tools/hub_build.sh
0.26-hubtest` (Docker; e371537a.., 3fb17c50.., 197ffc39.., c1094cf8..) exactly. **Not testable without pushing**: the
reusable workflow's call (permissions, `uses:` at v1), the attestation and its verification, softprops filling our
draft, `gh release download` from a draft, publishing the draft, the pages dispatch from publish, and the hub's sync
showing the key. Test plan (for the owner): (1) push this branch (no tag) and run release.yml from the Actions tab on it
with version `0.26-hubtest`: no release; fm1-build builds and signs the branch, and its artifact `packages` must
equal a local `tools/hub_build.sh 0.26-hubtest` (the sums above), `gh attestation verify` passing on each file. (2)
The whole tag flow away from ChoralRoot's releases: a public scratch repository (say Quixotic7/cr-release-test) with
this branch as its main, `git tag v0.26-hubtest1` pushed there: a draft, the signed files in it, publish's checks, a
pre-release (a suffix: no site); delete the repository afterwards (attestations stay in Sigstore's public log, naming
that repository). (3) The first real release after 0.26 with this release.yml; the hub's next sync should show the
key. Not pushed. No device.

**The dry run (step 1), and the merge (2026-10-20).** The branch pushed as `hub-signing` (12b0c40) and release.yml run

## 2026-10-25 (FX routing: the chain's order and feed, issue 21)

3y192. **The FX layer's Routing page: the order of the chorus, the delay and the reverb, and a feed that chains them
(GitHub issue 21: "re-orderable FX ... like how in the original OEM firmware you could re-arrange the FX order"; the
owner on the issue: "adding a new FX page for routing would work. Shows the entire routing").** ChoralRoot has no
serial chain to reorder: the drive is per part before the mix, the chorus, the delay and the reverb are three
parallel send buses whose returns are added into the mix, the master filter is after them. So a reorder alone would
change nothing, and the feature is two global parameters (`cr_fx.c XP_ORDER` 0..5, an index into `XF_ORD`'s six
permutations, and `XP_FEED` 0..127): the effect at position k (1, 2) takes, besides the parts' sends, the previous
block's return of the effect at position k-1, mono, times feed / 127. Feed 0, every unit's, is bit for bit 0.26 and
the order a picture; the drive and the filter stay where they are and are not on the page. The audio side (the
captures, the half-rate hooks, the glide, the dual-core split unchanged) is docs/FX.md "The routing".
**The page** (`cr_ui.c`): `CU_NFXP` 9, `CU_FXPG_ROUTE` 4 (cs.fx_sel stays, as on Sends and Filter), the picker's last
item "Routing", the row 1st / 2nd / 3rd / Feed. The three position cells (`FC_POS0` -12 .. -14, past the sends'
codes) read the effect's name at that place (`cu_fx_posname`; "Chorus", "Reverb" fit the knob row's value text, no
glyph: the row reads as the chain); a position's knob moves its effect one place a detent, clockwise right, clamped
at the ends (`cu_fx_move`: the permutation searched in `XF_ORD`); Feed is an `xfp` cell read in per cent as a send
(`cu_send_text`, the dry / wet glyph) at the knob's speed with OPT snap, and does not switch FX on. Traces "fx:
routing Chorus > Delay > Reverb feed 0" on entering the page and on every change; the dump's "screen reads" and
"session fx" show the page as any other. OPT + SELECT stays the click level.
**The session** (`cr_settings.*`): tags 35 `XF_ORDER` and 36 `XF_FEED` in `CSS_FXG`, written only off their defaults
as the filter's; `CSS_NFXP` 28 -> 29 by `rsv_fx`'s last four bytes (`css_fx_t` 128 bytes, `part[]` still at 196: a
static assert); `sel` 8 `CSS_FXE_ROUTE` (past `amt[]`, never its index: only `CSS_FXE[]`'s four effects index it).
An older firmware (0.22 .. 0.26) reads a record of up to 28 entries as before, skipping 35 / 36 (its chain is
parallel anyway) and `sel` 8 (no page of its own: the layer opens on Sends); a record of 29 (Order, Feed and all four
of the filter's off their defaults) makes it take the FX block's defaults, the precedent of 0.22's filter tags. The
FX block has no free entry left now (docs/SETTINGS.md).
**Checked**: `tools/emu/scripts/cr_fx_route.txt` with its block in `test_cr.sh` (the page's defaults, KNOB 1 +1 Delay
Chorus Reverb with a different screen, KNOB 2 -1 back, KNOB 3 -1 Chorus Reverb Delay, KNOB 1 -1 clamped, KNOB 4 +10
Feed 39 %, FX off / on: the page as it was); `test_persist.sh`'s session run sets Order 2 and Feed 14 on the Routing
page and finds them back after the relaunch (35=2 36=14, Delay 2's page still the one kept); `tests/cr_settings_test.c`
takes the new layout (29 entries, 128 bytes, 122 bytes of reserve); `tests/run_cr_draw.sh`. Not run on the device.
## 2026-10-25 (FX routing, second design: the chain, the boxes, the black keys)

3y193. **The Routing page redone as a signal flow (GitHub issue 21; the owner on 3y192: "I don't think this routing
works or addresses what was asked in the issue. You can only route delay chorus reverb and it's also a bit
confusing. I think the routing page should show a like route signal flow as boxes. Then on the routing page use the
black keys for shortcuts ... You can change the routing of an FX by selecting it with the black key shortcut and while
holding it down, it will raise up and you can use Knob 1 to move it around. We should be able to route and reorder
the Chorus, Delay, Reverb, and Filter. Since the previous routing was parallel bus sends, there should be an option
to change the routing to that.")** What changed from 3y192: the Feed is gone; the order is of FOUR blocks (Chorus,
Delay, Reverb, Filter: `cr_fx.c XP_ORDER` 0..23, `XF_ORD[24][4]`) and a mode `XP_RMODE`, 0 Parallel (0.26 bit for
bit, the default) or 1 Chain (a real serial path: the dry mix through the four in order, each effect's amount the
chord part's send; docs/FX.md "The routing"). **The page** is its own screen kind, `CR_K_ROUTE` (`cr_draw.c
cr_p_route`, modelled on FIZZ's MidiFX rail): Chain draws in, four coloured boxes numbered 1..4, out; Parallel the
three buses stacked into a sum, the Filter after it; a held block lifts with a shadow and arrows, the others slide
over (`CR_A_SLIDE`, the new `CR_A_LIFT`), it lands on release. The page owns the root keys (`cu_layer_keys`): C#4
Chorus, D#4 Delay, F#4 Reverb, G#4 Filter held + KNOB 1 (a place a detent, insert, clamped; Chain only: in Parallel
the order is not heard and KNOB 1 leaves it), A#4 tap and KNOB 2 the mode; the other keys are silent, the key LEDs
show the shortcuts (the LEDs have one colour: the four glow, A#4 middle, the held one lit). Traces "fx: routing
chain Chorus > Delay > Reverb > Filter", "fx: routing hold Delay" / "release Delay"; the emulator's dump prints
"screen route:". **The session**: tag 35 `XF_ORDER` 0..23, tag 36 now `XF_RMODE` 0..1 (`CSS_FXP_XF_RMODE`; 3y192's
two tags never shipped in a release, one dev unit; an old Feed above 1 is out of range and reads Parallel).
**Checked**: `tools/emu/scripts/cr_fx_route.txt` rewritten with its block in `test_cr.sh`, `test_persist.sh` (Order
2 and Chain set with the keys, back after the relaunch), four new states in `tests/run_cr_draw.sh`. Not run on the
device.

3y194. **In Parallel the Filter can go before the buses (GitHub issue 21, third pass; the owner, looking at the
Parallel picture: "With them in parallel it looks like filter always comes after the other FX, it would be good if you
could move the filter to put it before the FX which is probably the main reason people want to reorder.")** The
Filter at position 0 of `XP_ORDER` in Parallel puts it before the buses: the dry mix and the three sends are filtered
on core 0 before the effects stage (docs/FX.md "The routing", "The master filter"); anywhere else it is after the sum,
as before. **The page**: G#4 held + KNOB 1 in Parallel moves the Filter between its two places (`cr_ui.c
cu_fx_move`: anticlockwise to position 0, clockwise to position 3, the effects' order kept; a Filter already after
stays), arrows pointing to the other place; a held effect lifts and does not move. `cr_draw.c cr_p_route` draws in,
the Filter, then the fan, the stack and the sum 62 px to the right, and out; a move slides the whole picture between
the two. **Checked**: `cr_fx_route.txt` and its block in `test_cr.sh` (Filter first F C R D = Order 19, back after =
C R D F = Order 2, a held Delay moves nothing), the state "FX Routing (Parallel, Filter first)" in
`tests/run_cr_draw.sh`. Not run on the device.
